Blue Hour's board floor is 81 of 85 scene objects, moved down with the pack as its comment asked.** The plan put the "> 10 meshes" floor in `test/browser.mjs`; it is in `Tools/board-check/play-games.mjs`, where it read `> 10`, then `>= 100` of the 112 counted on 2026-09-24. The models are fewer meshes than the primitives they replace (the deer was ten meshes and is four primitives), so the count fell to 77 meshes and 8 instanced sets and the line failed, which is what "a deliberate cut below 100 should move this number with it" is for. At 81 it still catches the break it was written against: with every mesh added straight to the scene dropped, 65 are left. `browser.mjs`'s draw bound is its 300-call ceiling, and at the trailhead the count went from 47 to 39. *Blue Hour, B4.*

**#678. Golden Hour's prop pieces are drawn one `InstancedMesh` per variant, not merged per builder.** Instancing keeps each placed piece's matrix, so `test/props.mjs` can measure every post, boulder and plank against field.js, where a merged buffer would have to be taken apart again to be checked. The draw count still falls, from 98 to 39: `props.js` goes from 14 (a merge per builder, three wrack draws and five pool discs) to 28, one per variant, but the pier goes from 66 meshes (22 piles, 42 planks, two stringers) to 8 and the stone patches from 18 to 3. The shells, the stone in hand and each sandcastle are a group round one piece, because the game moves each of them alone. *Golden Hour, B6.*

**#679. A piece's origin goes back to where the builder's primitive had it, once, at load, and every placement keeps the builder's arithmetic.** `loadPieces()` moves each geometry down by half its own height, or by `ORIGIN_UP` where the primitive's origin was not its middle: the wrack shell (a cut sphere, 0.078 x s over its rim), the cockle (0.06 x s) and the whelk (0.681 x s), with the driftwood and the sandcastle at 0 because their builders stood them on the ground. So each builder passes its old position, turn and entry over `ref`, the builders' `rnd()` calls stay in their order, and the pier's deck stays `pierDeckY`. A variant is the entry's seed modulo the count; a plank has none, so it is its place along the pier, and the stone in hand is `0xcafe`'s. The cockles and whelks are the exception: the six cockles' seeds modulo 3 never come to 1, so `cockle-2` would have shipped unseen, and a find's variant is its place among its own kind instead. *Golden Hour, B6.*

**#680. Above the fog line Blue Hour's shape stands up and its eyes become a light on the slope below, going away.** The two visual beats change kind at #635's `summitKind`, decided where they are staged and held for the beat's life. The shape keeps everything the bear had (45 to 65 m ahead, the less-watched side, the head flipped down the fall line, gone when looked back at or walked to) and swaps the quadruped for a hunched upright figure 2.6 m tall, which is not a person because the figure at the lookout rail keeps that. The eyes become the row's own candidate: a cold-white headlamp halo 8 to 20 m to the less-watched side, at least 30 m off, swept from the fall line toward that side for the first ground 8 m under the walker (a straight 30 m left it 3 m below on the summit's shoulder, and a fall-line trace found nothing from the bench), walking downhill at 0.7 m/s for 9 to 13 s at no more than 0.4 opacity, the only other sign of a person on the mountain, leaving. A walker staring down both flanks gets nothing, as with the eyes. No save, no verbs, nothing that answers being looked for. Reversible by pointing both branches in `dread.js` at the woods' kinds; `test/browser.mjs` then fails the three kind lines. *Blue Hour, rank 43.*

**#681. Integer Foundry's model gaps were one gap, and closing it put the mergers in and kept the splitter out (Q48).** A merger pairs the first two packets to reach it, so fed by one line it pairs a value with itself and is a fixed single-input tile: Merge + doubles, Merge x squares, and `buildCosts` prices both as one tile of a chain, which keeps the snake bound that makes the budget tight. A two-line merge pairs by arrival timing, which is the layout, and stays out. The splitter's number now exists (sharing p operators saves p - 1 tiles, read off `tick()`) and it belongs to a pair of orders, not a floor, so `opBudget` keeps its even split: with x2 or Merge + owned no order on any floor costs more than a three-way share, and the credit would change no roll. Reversible by pointing `buildCosts` back at `OP_TILES`; `smoke-targets.mjs` then fails seven lines. *Integer Foundry, rank 43.*

**#682. Signal City's priority corridor follows its vehicle: once the player calls it, every box the car is handed to holds for it.** Real emergency pre-emption works this way, and it keeps the lesson as "call it early" rather than a key press per box. E and a click still call only a car nobody has called, and a car nobody called is held for nowhere, so the corridor stays the player's call. A ring or a blackout refuses the follow as it refuses E. The row expected Two Blocks and Main Street to change under play, and neither did: Main Street is one box and Two Blocks has no emergency vehicle. Both `--baseline --hand` tables match the pre-change tables exactly. The change only affects play in the Free Play district. Reversible by deleting the one `_followPriority` call in `_handoff`; `test/grid.mjs` then fails the box 2 and box 3 lines. *Signal City, R6.*

**#683. Blue Hour's props are built in their builder's own frame, and `budget.json`'s `ref.origin` says where the builder's origin sits in each grounded file.** Golden Hour's pieces were single primitives whose origin was half their height (#679); Blue Hour's are whole assemblies with their origin wherever the builder's arithmetic left it (the radio's is the cabin's, 1.7 m off). So `props.py` computes where that origin lands after grounding and exits 1 if it is more than 1 mm from `ref.origin`, and B6 subtracts it at load and keeps every `place()` as written. Broken from green: the bench's origin set 2 mm off stops the export, naming both numbers. *Blue Hour, B5.*

**#684. Where a Blue Hour builder loops over `LAYOUT`, its pieces are real entries, not averages.** The cairn stones are `LAYOUT.cairns[0]`'s first three stones at their own `r` (0.34, 0.3328, 0.2586) and seeds (0, 977, 1954), so each variant's box is one the game has actually drawn. The 14 markers are one builder, so they are two weatherings of it (a lean under 1.5 cm, a board tilt under 0.035 rad). The mushrooms are one shape at unit scale with two caps, the glowing one carrying the builder's emissive `#2a3a26`, which joined the palette. *Blue Hour, B5.*

**#685. Two style-sheet calls on Blue Hour's prop pack: `prop-small` is 1.1 m, and the cabin keeps its builder's box with a saltbox roof.** The radio's whip aerial makes it 1.07 m tall, and it belongs with the things the walker stands over, not in `prop`. The builder's roof is a three-sided `CylinderGeometry` that comes out as a lopsided prism, 4.40 m at z 0.95, where it drops as a vertical face, and 2.75 m at the back eave; the file keeps that height and that fall as a ridge at z 0.5, a short front pitch, gable ends and a stone chimney, 7.1% deeper and 2.4% taller than the builder. Reversible in `budget.json` and `cabin()`. *Blue Hour, B5.*

**#686. The Fourth Quarter's bar pieces are built in metres at the Corner Tap's `world.js` box, and face +Z.** The fit-out's `w`, `h` and `d` are the same in all four rooms, so B4's scale of `f.w`, `f.h`, `f.d` over the file's box is 1. +Z is glTF's own front and the side a fit-out block at `rotY` 0 shows the room, so `f.rotY` goes on unchanged, with no half turn, unlike Blue Hour's animals (#675). Files go in `models/` at the project root, beside `textures/` and `audio/`. *The Fourth Quarter, B1.*

**#687. A Fourth Quarter model's material is one of two kinds, and the validator enforces both.** A `mat()` key from `js/materials.js` carries that material's placeholder colour and UVs spanning 0 to 1 per face, so the game can put its own tiered material on by name. A `flat-<what>` material is white with its colour in `COLOR_0`. The two never share an object, because Blender's exporter writes `COLOR_0` onto every primitive of a mesh once one material reads it; `mesh_object()` refuses the mix. `export_image_format` is `'AUTO'`: `'NONE'` silently drops vertex colour under 5.2.2, and broken on purpose that failed two validator lines and the loader's ("vertexColors false, colour #e7e7e7"). *The Fourth Quarter, B1.*

**#688. The Fourth Quarter's byte caps are triangles x 128 + 8 KiB, and its classes are `fixture`, `counter`, `bottle` and `frame`.** The plan named four triangle caps and no byte caps. A 240-triangle crate is 19,836 bytes, so 128 bytes a triangle plus a header allowance leaves each class room without letting a file carry a texture it should not. Longest sides are 2.6, 2.5, 0.5 and 2.5 m. The kick, shelves and sill are `counter`, the cans are `bottle`, and the palette is the 18 `flat()` colours in `world.js`; the burners' `glow()` is B3's call. *The Fourth Quarter, B1.*

**#689. The Fourth Quarter's stove burners stay the game's `glow()` discs; the stove model is the body they sit on.** A burner is an emissive, and in this pipeline a model's colour is never emissive (#687's two material kinds have no third). So `world.js` keeps drawing the four discs at `f.h + 0.01`, and `stove.glb` carries a grate under each spot with its top at the block's 0.95. Reversible by giving `common.py` an emissive kind and adding it to `validate.mjs`. *The Fourth Quarter, B3.*

**#690. Whatever `world.js` sizes from the room is a unit piece that B4 repeats or stretches along x.** The counter is two 0.5 m `counter-end`s, the west one mirrored with `scale.x = -1`, and `counter-mid`, one metre, filling `desc.bar.len - 1`. The kick and both shelves are one-metre pieces, and the sill is built at the Corner Tap's 1.9 m. `barLen` runs from 7 to 12 m over the four rooms, which no class extent can hold, and B1 had already said the counter was ends and a middle. A middle has no stile at its edges, so a run of them shows no doubled joint. *The Fourth Quarter, B3.*

**#691. Every Aphelion model material is a key of `M` in `src/ship.js`, with `M`'s numbers, and nothing else.** The game draws every surface from that table, with no texture and no vertex colour, so there is no flat- kind, no `COLOR_0` and no UV. `M` is read from `ship.js` as text by `common.py`, `validate.mjs` and the loader test, because the file imports three. A `panel` slot carries `M.panel`'s emissive at its 0.25, and the wiring row gives each system its own clone, so the power state still dims it. A piece keeps `ship.js`'s box even where it floats: the console's base is 0.15 m above the deck, and B4 puts it there. *Aphelion, B1.*

**#692. Blue Hour's props are one instanced set per material per variant, and the board's floor for the mountain is 91 of 95.** Merged as #689 in PR #458 while PR #459 was citing #689 to #691 for The Fourth Quarter and Aphelion, which had not reached this file yet; Blue Hour's three moved up to #692 to #694 so theirs could land at the numbers their code already cites. Golden Hour's #678, for the same reason: an instance keeps each placement's matrix, so `test/props.mjs` can read counts and scales back off the scene. The markers go from two merged meshes to four sets, the cairns from one to three, the mushrooms from three to four (a file each for the plain and the glowing clusters, stem and cap each). The assemblies are a group of one mesh per material. The mountain went from 79 meshes and 8 sets to 79 and 16, so `play-games.mjs`'s floor rose from 81 to 91, the same four under the count; with the props group dropped it reads 70 and fails. The draw count in `test/browser.mjs` is 47, under its 300. *Blue Hour, B6.*

**#693. A marker's variant is its place along the trail, and a cairn stone's is its place in its cairn.** A marker has no seed, so `marker-1` and `marker-2` alternate by index (#679's rule for a plank). The three stones are `LAYOUT.cairns[0]`'s first three (#684), so stone s is variant s mod 3 and each cairn's bottom stone is the one built as a bottom stone; a stone scales by its own r over its variant's `ref.r`. A variant weathered at another stone's seed can put a corner up to 2 x 0.18 x r out, 0.137 m at r 0.38, which is the cairns' tolerance in the test; they read 0.094. *Blue Hour, B6.*

**#694. Blue Hour's lit props take the file's own material, not the builders' Lambert.** GLTFLoader gives a lit glTF material a `MeshStandardMaterial`, and the pack writes every one at metallic 0 and roughness 0.85 to 0.95, which reads as matte as Lambert did; the animals took theirs the same way (B4), as did Golden Hour's pieces. Converting at load would be a second copy of the palette to keep in step. The unlit four (blazes, panes, window, lens) arrive as `MeshBasicMaterial`, and `update()` lerps the cabin `window`'s own material. Reversible in `loadPieces()`. *Blue Hour, B6.*

**#695. The Fourth Quarter's bar pack loads at `world.js`'s top-level await, and a keyed part takes `mat(key)` at build time, not at load.** `loadPieces()` runs when `world.js` is first imported, before `main.js` has called `initTextures()`, so swapping `mat()` in there would take the default tier and `pickTier()` would choose nothing. `pieceGroup()` asks `world.js` for each part's material as it builds, and `mat()` now names its material for its key, so a swapped part keeps the name the file gave its slot. `loadPieces()` waits for every file to settle before it rejects on the first, in `PIECES` order; `test/bar.mjs` cannot tell that from a `Promise.all` and says so. *The Fourth Quarter, B4.*

**#696. A run of one-metre pieces is as many whole metres as divide it most nearly, each stretched to fit.** The counter's middle, the kick, both shelves: 6 of them for a 6 m middle, 2 for the kitchen's 2.2 m shelf at 1.1 m each. That keeps a keyed texture near the one-metre density its box UVs were built at, where one stretched piece would smear a 12 m bar's grain twelve times. The sill is one piece scaled in x by the window's width over 1.9 m, and a fit-out block by its own w, h, d over the file's box, which is 1 in every room there is. The counter scales in z by `desc.bar.depth` over 0.75, also 1 everywhere. *The Fourth Quarter, B4.*

**#697. `test/bar.mjs` holds every piece to 2 cm of the builder's box, not the 10% `validate.mjs` allows.** The boxes are `test/fixtures/bar-builders.json`, measured once off `world.js` at 3a5db43 in all four rooms. The files were built at those boxes, so all but two land exactly: the stool stands 1 cm taller (its leg started 1 cm up in `stool()`) and the stove's grates reach 9 mm past its block. At 10% a bottle or a tap put in at its centre height would have passed; at 2 cm it fails "worst 0.175 m". Whether the west counter end is mirrored has its own line, because a mirrored end has the same box. *The Fourth Quarter, B4.*

**#698. Bell to Bell's pack carries its colour in the file, named for a palette entry: the game's `PALETTE` and the four Kenney colours.** The game loads a model's own materials (`loadStatic`), so unlike The Fourth Quarter and Aphelion there is no key for a wiring row to swap. The palette is `PALETTE`'s ten flat colours in `src/world/materials.js` plus the kit's `wood` f3cca8, `metal` dfeaec, `metalMedium` a3b6b6 and `carpet` f9a39e, read off `desk.glb`, `bookcaseClosedDoors.glb`, `bookcaseOpen.glb` and `chairDesk.glb`. Classes: `classroom` 2,000 triangles and a 1,024 px map, the room's own sets' size; `desktop` 600 and 512 px (#621). An image is a loose file beside the `.gltf`, as the Poly Haven props' are, because the recipe keeps them loose (#624). Bytes are the `.gltf` and `.bin` together. *Bell to Bell, B1.*

**#699. Bell to Bell's validator checks the recipe's `.glb` against its `.gltf` by accessor corners.** It cannot decode meshopt with no dependency, so the `.glb`'s box is each POSITION accessor's min and max through its node's matrix, dequantized, and has to match the `.gltf`'s vertex box to 1 cm, with the same triangles, materials and image URIs. EXT_meshopt_compression is allowed there and fails in what Blender wrote. Checked on two real recipe outputs, the clipboard and the wall clock, against their sources at 52d2a59: every `.glb` line passed through `KHR_mesh_quantization`'s node scales. *Bell to Bell, B1.*

**#700. Aphelion's pieces are built in `src/ship.js`'s own frame, and `budget.json` says where each origin goes.** `ship.py`'s `tbox()` takes `ship.js`'s `box()` arguments as written, so a builder reads line for line against the code it replaces, and each item's `at` is the centre of its base in the game's frame (the tray's group, the POI group, or an offset from `systems.json`'s `panel.pos`), which `ship.py` prints on every run. The wiring row sets a position and no rotation, except on the system panel: it alone is built facing +Z, the file's front, so `systems.json`'s `rotY`, which `ship.js` never read, turns it to its wall. *Aphelion, B3.*

**#701. What `ship.js` builds from rooms.json stays built from it, and the pack is the eleven things standing in it.** The floor, ceiling, walls, partitions, doorways and window panes are a description of `rooms.json` (#646's Fourth Quarter walls), as are the starfield and the sun; the engine glows and the gauge strips are `MeshBasicMaterial` colours with no key of `M`, and stay the game's. All three EVA points of interest draw one satellite today, so the pack is one `satellite.glb` every POI takes; a cargo pod and an escape pod that look like themselves are a design row. `M` gains `solar`, the wings' inline material with its numbers (0x2a3d66, 0.3, 0.5), and the POI loop uses it; the `interior` class's longest side goes from 4.5 to 6 m for the 5.6 m pipe run, which is interior by any reading. *Aphelion, B3.*

**#702. Bell to Bell's classroom pack is the window and the objective board.** Of `room.json`'s fixtures: the room shell stays the room (#646); the whiteboard is B1's; the four posters are already dressed by `assets.json`'s `art` frame and paintings; and the rug stays a 2 cm slab, because it carries the game's tiled `wool_boucle` set at 1.6 m and a flat `rug` file would trade a texture for a colour (a map in the pack would be a second copy of that set to keep in step). One `window` file serves all three, built facing +Z at 1.5 x 1.2 x 0.06 and turned -pi/2 onto the right wall; the objective board keeps its box, face 1.5 cm back, so board.js's canvas still stands 2 cm clear. Both are `classroom`. Glass carries `createMaterials()`'s 0.75, blended, from `budget.json`'s new `opacity`, and `validate.mjs` holds every material to its alpha. The screens, the students' box bodies, the tell meshes and the fallback boxes behind the Kenney desks, chairs and bookshelf stay. *Bell to Bell, B2.*

**#703. A piece held to 2 cm of its builder's box may carry named dressing past it, never a looser tolerance.** Aphelion's bed and hull land exactly at their `at` but reach 3 cm past `ship.js`'s boxes: the blanket fold stands over the frame's top, and the hull's seam bands (7.06 x 4.06 round a 7 x 4 body) reach under it, with the pod bands 2 cm past the pods on x. `test/ship.mjs`'s `DRESSING` adds those to the builder's faces before the 2 cm is held, so a piece put in at the wrong height still fails by its own number. *Aphelion, B4.*

**#704. A mesh recipe whose files are too small for the raw-under-gzipped rule is held to raw under raw and a stated gzip slack.** At 72 to 84 triangles the `.glb`'s JSON chunk is 2,880 to 3,496 of its 3,796 to 4,464 bytes, and the `.gltf` and `.bin` it replaces gzip to 1,394 to 1,456 together, so no raw output can come in under them. `bell-to-bell-blender` sets `gzipSlack: 256`: each output is under its source raw (the host that does not gzip) and within 256 bytes of it gzipped (the host that does). Measured, 61 to 126 bytes more a file, for one request where there were two. A recipe without the field keeps the header's rule. *Bell to Bell, B3.*

**#705. Aphelion's interactable is a mesh or a whole piece, and the pick raycasts into groups.** The bed, the tray and the satellite are their `bed`, `soil` and `sat` parts; the hatches and the system panels are the whole piece, since every part of a door is the door. `pickInteractable()` intersects recursively and walks up from the hit to the first eligible object. `M`'s materials are named for their keys and exported, so a test can hold every part to `M[name]` by identity. *Aphelion, B4.*

**#706. Bell to Bell hangs a Blender piece on its fixture from `assets.json`'s `fixtures`, and keeps the fixture's box as the fallback.** A fixture id names a model and a `rotY`; `room.js`'s `hangPiece()` puts the piece at the fixture's base, unscaled, and `dressFixture()` registers it with the fixture's thermal hex and hides the box, which comes back with a warning if the file fails, as `dressWithModel()` has always done in this game. A missing file still fails suite lines (`props.mjs`, and `assets.mjs`'s "referenced but missing"), so the fallback is never silent in CI (#646). The window's turn is tested against the room, not against the `rotY` it reads. *Bell to Bell, B3.*

**#707. Blender runs on two machines, and the gate says which: `blender` for basic headless work (huginn or Devon's Windows machine), `blender-gpu` for the full feature set (the Windows machine only).** Devon's instruction, 2026-09-29. Since that day huginn has Blender 5.2.2 LTS on PATH, but its Cycles has only a CPU (no HIP), its Eevee a Vega iGPU, and it has 14 GB of RAM with little swap free. Building meshes, glTF export and small Workbench or CPU Cycles sheets run there; large lit renders do not, so the site's dioramas (ranks 23 and 24) are gated `blender-gpu` and every other Blender row stays `blender`. A lit render's bytes differ between machines, so one pack renders on one machine and its PR names which. *All projects; rule 4.*

---

**#707. The Conversion Codex converts a PF1e number by its tier, not its value, and a CR becomes the same-numbered level.** Each stat is placed against PF1e's Monster Statistics by CR (anchored to measured Bestiary medians below CR 3, where Table 1-1 runs 2 to 3 points low), and the PF2e creature takes the GM Core value at the same continuous tier for its level. Level = CR from CR 1 up, 0 for CR 1/2, -1 below: of 165 monsters printed in both editions at CR 1+, 117 kept their number. `converter-convert.test.mjs` measures eleven conversions against the printed PF2e monster in `data/npcs`, so a change to either rule is judged against Paizo rather than against itself. *Pathfinder Converter, 2026-09-29.*

**#708. The PF1e spell list and the spell map live in `converter-assets/data/`, and the PF2e side is read from `Pathfinder/data/spell.json`, not copied.** `Pathfinder/data/` is the Archive's and only its script writes there (#350), so the converter's own data sits in its own folder; `converter-spells.test.mjs` asserts every `data/spell.json` field it reads and every PF2e name the map gives, as data/README.md asks of a reader. A regeneration that renames a spell turns that suite red. *Pathfinder Converter, 2026-09-29.*

**#709. The Conversion Codex keeps nothing in the browser.** A pasted stat block lives only in the page, so there is no storage key to protect under #36; a spell's hash route (`#spell/<name>`) is the only state that survives a reload. Saving a converted creature is a feature someone should ask for first. *Pathfinder Converter, 2026-09-29.*

**#711. The Conversion Codex's hp anchor is tier 2.25, a quarter above moderate.** At moderate, 7 of the 11 printed monsters in `converter-convert.test.mjs` came out under (troll 86 against 115, balor 375 against 480), mean error 20.0%, bias -4.1%. Of 2, 2.1, 2.2, 2.25, 2.3, 2.4, 2.5 and 3, tier 2.25 measured least on both (19.0%, +0.5%), and the wider answer key's -7.7% bias at moderate pointed the same way; the suite now holds hp error to 19.5% and bias to 3%, which moderate and 2.5 each fail. *Pathfinder Converter, 2026-09-29.*

**#712. The Conversion Codex anchors hp at low below CR 1 and at 2.5 from CR 1 up, replacing #711's 2.25.** Paizo builds its level -1 mooks on low Hit Points (Skeleton Guard 4, Goblin Warrior 6), so five more CR 1/4 to 1/2 pairs went into `converter-convert.test.mjs`: at 2.25 the six ran +26.8% (goblin, kobold and skeleton 43% to 50% over), and at low 12.5% error and -2.5% bias, the least of the six anchors tried. With the goblin out of the CR 1+ evidence, those ten measured 15.9% and -4.5% bias at 2.25 and 14.8% and +1.0% at 2.5; #711 had passed on 2.5 only because it pushed the goblin past its 50% bound. The hp explanation says the low landing is on purpose. *Pathfinder Converter, 2026-09-29.*

**#713. The Conversion Codex anchors hp at 2.2 at CR 1 and 2, between #712's low below CR 1 and 2.5 from CR 3 up.** Ten CR 1 and 2 Bestiary monsters from aonprd.com became pairs in `converter-convert.test.mjs` (ghoul against Monster Core's Ghoul Stalker; giant frog, darkmantle, homunculus, boar, crocodile, giant ant, dretch, choker, hippogriff by name), which made eleven at CR 1 and 2 with the wolf. At #712's 2.5 those eleven ran +7.4% bias and 19.5% error (ghoul 44% over, darkmantle and crocodile 30%), while the nine from CR 3 up held at +1.6%. With CR 1 to 2 on its own anchor: 2.1 ran -2.0%, 2.15 -0.8%, 2.2 +0.2%, 2.25 +0.9%, 2.3 +3.2%. 2.2 is taken because it zeroes the bias; the bias over eleven pairs has a standard error of about 6.4% (their errors spread 21%), so 2.1 to 2.25 are equally supported. One anchor of 2.25 for every CR from 1 up (#711's value) was the alternative: 15.9% error over all 26 against 15.7% here, but it put CR 3+ at -3.6%. The split is at CR 3 because that is where `pf1Anchors` stops using measured PF1e medians and starts using Table 1-1, and PF1e has no CR between 2 and 3, so the switch never falls inside a level. *Pathfinder Converter, 2026-09-29.*

**#714. The Conversion Codex anchors hp at 2.2 from CR 1 up, folding #713's 2.5 from CR 3 into its 2.2 at CR 1 and 2.** Twelve CR 3 to 6 Bestiary monsters from aonprd.com became pairs (dire wolf, doppelganger, rust monster, hell hound, gargoyle, griffon, mimic, basilisk, gibbering mouther, djinni, ettin, wyvern), which made twenty-one from CR 3 up. At 2.5 those twenty-one ran +7.9% bias and 20.1% error; 2.0 ran -3.6%, 2.1 -1.4%, 2.15 -0.3%, 2.2 +0.8%, 2.25 +2.0%, 2.3 +3.1%. The gargoyle is +65% at 2.2 on its own, because Monster Core prints it at 40 hp, under level 4's low row, and gives it physical resistance 5; without it zero bias falls near 2.3. Their errors spread 23%, a standard error of about 5%, so 2.1 to 2.3 are equally supported and the CR 3 split no longer separates anything. One anchor at 2.15 measured 16.4% error over all 38 pairs, at 2.2 16.5%; 2.2 is taken because it leaves every CR 1 and 2 conversion unchanged. The gargoyle stays in the averages and is excused only from the per-monster 50% hp bound. *Pathfinder Converter, 2026-09-29.*

**#715. The Conversion Codex reads a PF1e construct's Fortitude no lower than moderate.** A PF1e construct has no Con, so its Fort is a bare base save: the stone golem's +4 at CR 11 and the iron golem's +6 at CR 13 read as terrible and converted to 15 and 18, against Paizo's 24 and 26. Of the 83 constructs `data/npcs` holds from the Bestiaries and Monster Core, 40 print high or extreme Fortitude, 30 moderate (20 of them the soulbound doll's variants) and 13 low, and every golem but wood, glass and mithral is high or better. A floor at high zeroes both golems but puts the homunculus, printed at low, 8 over; moderate leaves the golems 3 under and the homunculus 5 over. Reflex and Will are left as read. *Pathfinder Converter, 2026-09-30.*

**#716. The Conversion Codex keeps hp at 2.2 from CR 7 up; high CRs get a bound, not an anchor.** Ten CR 7 to 14 monsters became pairs (chuul, medusa, hill giant, gorgon, frost giant, fire giant, stone golem, glabrezu, nalfeshnee from aonprd.com, and the erinyes fixture already on disk against Erinys), making fourteen from CR 7 up with the succubus, lich, iron golem and balor. The four had run -6% at 2.2; the fourteen run -0.3% bias and 14.5% error, with 2.1 at -2.7% and 2.3 at +2.1%. Their errors spread 17%, a standard error of about 4.4%, so the four's -6% was noise. `converter-convert.test.mjs` holds CR 7 up to 2% bias, and the overall error bound goes from 16.6% to 16.2% so that it still fails 2.25 (16.4% over the 48 pairs). *Pathfinder Converter, 2026-09-30.*

**#717. Orbital's sprite pipeline is the repo's first 2D one, copied from Aphelion's `common.py` and not from Signal City's, and the later 2D copies start from Orbital's.** The common plan named Signal City's renderer as the source of every 2D copy (#643), but Signal City's B1 (rank 4) had not shipped when Orbital's rows came up, and a plan is not a file to copy from. `Projects/orbital/tools/blender/common.py` is Aphelion's at db06e5f with the glTF export replaced by a sprite renderer: one scene, every body in its own cell of a grid at one Blender unit per frame pixel, one orthographic camera straight down, a transparent film, the frames packed by the render itself and the atlas written from the grid. The atlas is the common plan's shape plus one field, `r`, the body's radius in frame pixels, because a frame of a round body is scaled by a radius and not by a box. Each 2D project still keeps its own copy (#643). *Orbital, B1.*

**#718. Orbital's frames are 256 px square, every body drawn to a 120 px extent, and the game scales a frame by `(R * view.s) / r`.** The frame is sized from the largest body a shipped level draws: the star at r 78 on the 1000 x 640 playfield is 125 screen pixels of radius at 1080p, so a 120 px frame radius is drawn at about its own size there and scaled down on everything smaller (a 4K screen at DPR 1 would upscale it twice, and the bodies are soft discs the glow already blurs). Each type's `r` is set so its outermost drawn thing, not its physics radius, lands at 120: planet and star 120, wormhole 116 (the outer dashed ring), rock 108 (the farthest bump), booster 100 (the tail chevron reaches 1.15 R behind the centre; at 116 it ran 134 px out and six pixels into the wormhole's frame), repulse 80 (the dashed ring at 1.5 R), black hole 62 (the outer accretion ring at 1.9 R). `validate.mjs` holds the rendered reach to 10% of `budget.json`'s extent and under the frame's half side less a 4 px margin, so a frame turned in the game never shows a neighbour. *Orbital, B1.*

**#719. Orbital renders with Workbench, the Standard view transform, a two-stop exposure, the studio light turned 90 degrees, and every stamp flag off.** Workbench and not EEVEE, because the common plan's rerun has to be byte-stable and Workbench has no sampler to seed; its 8-sample anti-aliasing is a fixed pattern. Standard and not AgX so a palette hex renders as itself. Under the Default studio light a ball's mean came out at half its material's sRGB (the planet's #63d8ff to #366273), and two stops of exposure put the mean back on the hex (#6db8ca) with the key side clipping toward `drawBody`'s light stop, which is how the game shades it; unturned, that key lit every ball from the lower right, and 90 degrees puts it upper left, where `drawBody`'s gradient centre sits (-0.3 R, -0.3 R). The stamp: three renders with identical pixels hashed three ways, because Blender writes its stamp into the PNG as tEXt chunks and two of them, Date and RenderTime, change every run; with every `use_stamp_*` flag off the file has no text chunk and three renders hash the same. *Orbital, B1.*

**#720. A frame is `drawBody` without the glow, at spin 0, flow 0 and dir 0, in `COLOR` and `drawBody`'s own stops, and the wiring row rotates it.** The glow stays the game's (BACKLOG's B1 line), so nothing in a frame is a `glowCircle`; the black hole's `COLOR` (#7c5cff) is its glow's colour and nothing in its frame wears it, which is why its `hue` check is off. Every other colour is lifted from `drawBody`: the gradient stops (the planet's cloud banks in #c9f2ff, the star's spots in #c9701a, the black hole's core in #05010f, the wormhole's core in #04211d) and the strokes (the repulsor's ring in rgba(255,150,225,.7), the accretion rings in rgba(255,230,255,.85) and rgba(200,180,255,.5), the core's edge in rgba(160,140,255,.9)), with their alphas rendered as material alpha into the PNG. Stroke widths and dash lengths are the game's numbers at DPR 1 scaled by the frame's pixels per world unit at the type's default radius in `levelcode.js`. The rings' spin, the booster's flow and its `dir` are motion, and a still cannot carry motion: B3 rotates the frame where `drawBody` rotates the drawing and draws the chevrons' flow as a still. *Orbital, B2.*

**#721. Signal City's sprite frames are Cycles on the CPU, one render a frame, packed by the pipeline's own PNG writer, and the atlas wraps its frames with the sheet's numbers.** 128 samples, seed 0, adaptive sampling and the denoiser off, the Standard view: two full runs of 60 frames gave one hash, and `-t 2` against `-t 4` gave one too. OpenImageDenoise was also stable, and it took only 9% off the PNG while softening the seams and the lettering. Workbench was stable but cannot put the light where `sprites.js` puts its gloss. The atlas is `{ sheet: { w, h, ppm, pad }, frames: {...} }` rather than the common plan's bare frame map, because the game needs `ppm` to scale a frame. The crown on a hull is its corner polygon moved in and rounded again at the radius less the inset, not `bmesh.ops.inset_region`, which threw granny's vertices out to x = -4.87 on a 2.9 m half-length and seven hulls with them. Orbital's pipeline (#717) merged first and is the one later 2D copies start from; this one differs where a car needs it to (a lit render for the gloss, a frame per render at `spriteFor`'s own size) and its `render_frame`, `pack` and `write_png` are there to take if a copy wants them. *Signal City, B1.*

**#722. Signal City's car sheet is 36 px a metre, and a frame is drawn only while `spriteFor` asks for 12 to 36.** The closest zoom the game draws is 27 px/m on screen, and `spriteFor` renders at twice the map scale, so the true ceiling is 54. At 54 four frames came to 107 KB, which puts the sheet near 1.7 MB. At 36 the 60 frames are 883,946 bytes, and 36 covers every default zoom on a dpr-2 screen and two thirds of the wheel's range. Above 36 the vector car stays sharp where an upscaled frame would blur. Below 12 a car is under 55 sprite pixels long, and a district's 6 px car (#617) gains nothing from a render. Reversible by changing `budget.json`'s `ppm` and rerunning `cars.py`. *Signal City, B1.*

**#723. A car's palettes are separate frames, not a body and a tint mask; rideshare has four states and emergency two; the trailer gets its own four.** A palette carries body, glass and accent, and the trailer paints its box in the cab's accent and its stripe in the cab's body, so a single tint channel could not recolour any of them. The states are the ones `spriteFor` actually caches: rideshare's pulse at t = 0, 0.25, 0.5 and 0.75, and the light bar's two halves, since t = 0.5 draws what t = 0 does. `render.js` draws the trailer every frame without a cache, so it is in the sheet for B3 to use. *Signal City, B2.*

**#724. SkyWings 64 moves in as `Projects/skywings64/`, with no history.** It was built outside any repository at `~/projects/skywings64` and Devon asked on 2026-09-30 for it to live here and be developed here from now on. It vendors its own three.js r160 (#17) and only the 19 addon files its import graph reaches, found by walking every static and dynamic import from `src/` and cross-checked by grepping for `three/addons/`: 23 MB of addons became 290 KB. A new addon import means copying that one file from r160. *SkyWings 64, 2026-09-30.*

**#725. SkyWings 64's suite steps the game synchronously and never waits on a frame.** `window.__qa.sim(seconds, ctrl)` calls `game.update` in a loop with a fixed dt, and `game.update` polls the keyboard, gamepad and touch state itself, so every beat in `test/browser.mjs` is a sim beat rather than a real-time one and a SwiftShader frame rate cannot decide it (#53). The first version waited on `requestAnimationFrame` and got through four of seventeen input beats in 150 s of virtual time. The same change moved the pause debounce from `performance.now()` to game time, which the suite caught: under a slow frame the wall-clock window swallowed Start and the touch pause button. *SkyWings 64, 2026-09-30.*

**#726. SkyWings 64's GLB colours are converted sRGB to linear on load, not re-baked.** The Blender scripts set Principled Base Color from sRGB picks, so every exported `baseColorFactor` was sRGB in a linear slot and the eleven models rendered washed out (red roofs came out salmon). `src/core/models.js` converts each GLB material once as it loads. Re-exporting would need the AO bakes redone for all eleven; if the scripts are ever fixed at the source, this conversion has to go in the same change. *SkyWings 64, 2026-09-30.*

**#727. A render is held to its calibration, size, walk lanes and bytes, not to its hash.** Cycles output with the denoiser is not byte-stable across runs or machines, and #652's one-hash rule is for .glb files. `validate.mjs` checks that the plate exists, is 3200 x 1800, is within 460,800 bytes, has median luminance of 14 or more in the walk lanes (room y 644 to 672 and 818 to 848) so a `#0c0803` silhouette reads, and that `assets/tavern/` holds no stray file; with `--rendered` it adds nine markers held to 2 room units. Shipped plate: 171,090 bytes, lanes 36.0 and 34.0, worst marker 0.42 units. *The site, 2026-09-30.*

**#728. The procedural room stays as the fallback.** `paintRoom()` draws the plate only after `decode()` resolves, and draws its rectangles until then and whenever the load fails, so the first frame is never blank. Broken on purpose: the plate URL pointed at a missing file, and the procedural room painted in full. *The site, 2026-09-30.*

**#729. The rim light stays canvas, along with the fire, glow, embers, motes, candles, lantern, door night wash and the near beams.** Each of them moves, glows, or sits in front of a walker, so none can be baked. The plate holds the fire's light and no fire; its firebox is black. *The site, 2026-09-30.*

**#730. The plate has non-square pixels, and calibration renders at the plate's size.** The plan's 3200 x 1800 (16:9) covers a 2000 x 1300 room frame (20:13), and the first `render()` would have cropped it to y -112.5 to 1012.5. Pixels are 1.156 times taller than wide and the page draws the plate back onto the 2000 x 1300 rect, so calibrating at the plate's own size tests the mapping that ships. *The site, 2026-09-30.*

**#731. Camera distance 19.5 m is pinned by the canvas's 0.28 tabletop ellipse, to 0.01.** Wall markers land 1:1 at any distance and cannot see it: a calibration rendered at 25 m passed all nine markers and failed only the disc (0.219). `validate.mjs` holds it both as arithmetic from budget.json and as a rendered disc (0.278). *The site, 2026-09-30.*

**#732. The tables stay in the plate for T1, and `paintTable()` re-lays the plate's own table pixels in the depth sort.** It clips them to the table's shape, so a far walker still goes behind a table. T2 moves the tables to an alpha sheet and `plateTable()` goes. *The site, 2026-09-30.*

**#733. The tavern plates are rendered on huginn: CPU Cycles, 128 samples with the denoiser, `-t 4`, WebP quality 92.** The room plate took 31.5 minutes and 3.37 GB peak. 256 samples would have doubled the time for no visible gain over the 96-sample previews, and quality 82 smeared the plaster and grain. One pack's plates come from one machine (#707), so T2 and T3 render on huginn too; `run.mjs` waits while any other Blender process runs. *The site, 2026-09-30.*

**#734. The tavern set's T1 session was launched on Fable 5.1 against #638, and it delegated the work rather than stopping.** It could not switch itself, so it gave the build to the `builder` agent (Opus 5.5) and this record to `scribe` (Sonnet 5), and kept only the lead role (#533). This records what one session did, not a change to #638: whether a session launched that way should stop instead is Devon's call. *The site, 2026-09-30.*

**#735. Orbital's first frame waits for the body sheet, and a sheet that will not load stops the game with the reason on its intro card.** `startFrames()` in `game.js` asks for the first animation frame only once `loadSprites()` has the PNG and an atlas with a frame for every `COLOR` key; on a failure it writes the file and the reason into `#linkErr`, puts the intro card back up with Begin disabled, and sends the same line to the console. `drawBody`'s gradients are deleted, so there is nothing to fall back to (#646). *Orbital, B3.*

**#736. The glow is drawn over the frame for six bodies and under it for the black hole.** The row says over, and over is right where the frame is a lit ball or a line drawing: the glow is what the frame lacks (#720). The black hole is the one body whose old drawing put a dark core over its glow. Its core's centre pixel was 0,0,5 in the gradients; the frame alone puts 69,70,75 there, and the glow over the frame 99,82,174, a violet disc. Under the frame it stays the frame's grey. The wormhole was measured the same way and keeps the row's order: 6,36,32 before, 65,73,75 with the glow under, 84,164,150 with it over, and teal is nearer what it was than grey. The grey itself is B2's render (Workbench lights a #05010f ball up to grey at two stops), and darkening it is a Blender rerun, not wiring. *Orbital, B3.*

**#737. A frame turns as one piece: the black hole at twice the clock, the wormhole at three times, the booster by `dir`, and the chevrons stand still.** Those are `drawBody`'s own numbers (`spin * 2`, `spin * 3`, `b.dir`). Two things the gradients did are gone with them. The wormhole's middle ring never turned, because `ctx.rotate` accumulated across the three rings and the second one's `-3` cancelled the first one's `3`; one frame cannot hold one ring still, so all three turn together. The booster's chevrons flowed along `dir` on `t * 0.004`; the frame is flow 0 (#720). The black hole's core turns with its rings, and its lit side with it. Under reduced motion the clock is 0, as before. *Orbital, B3.*

**#738. Throneshard came in as files, not history, and every name in it is its own.** It was built in a private local repo as a clone of a commercial MOBA; Devon asked for it here under a legally distinct name. The title, all 14 heroes, their 56 abilities, 81 of the 111 items (the other 30 are plain nouns such as Chainmail and Claymore), the teams, the base core, the boss, the announcer's streak lines and every code id, file name and storage key were renamed before the first commit here. The old history and the old-to-new map stay in the private repo, because every file here is a public URL and a map would tie the two back together. *Throneshard, 2026-09-30.*

**#739. Throneshard's storage keys are `throneshard.*`, set before it first shipped, so #36 is not broken.** The keys were renamed from the private build's prefix in the same change that brought the game here. No player has ever held a save under either prefix on this site. From this merge on #36 applies to them as to every other key. *Throneshard, 2026-09-30.*

**#740. Throneshard dropped Vite for an importmap and a vendored three r186.** `three` maps to `./libs/three.module.js` and the prefix `three/examples/jsm/` to `./libs/addons/`, which holds only the 19 addon files the game imports and their own imports, so no source file needed its import lines touched. The four `import.meta.env.BASE_URL` sites became relative paths, `import.meta.glob` became a plain import, and the CSS import became a `<link>`. The Playwright dev tools under `tools/scripts/` are not vendored and not in CI. *Throneshard, 2026-09-30.*

**#741. Throneshard's browser suite plays one whole bot match with a fixed step and rendering stubbed.** Under the harness's software GL a rendered frame costs far more than a tick, so `test/browser.mjs` stops the animation loop, stubs `renderer.render` and calls `game.tick()` at `fixedDt = 0.05` until the match ends or 70 game minutes pass. Nothing in it is timed (#53). The end screen is waited for on the DOM, because `UI.js` raises it on a 2.5 s wall-clock `setTimeout`. *Throneshard, 2026-09-30.*

**#742. Orbital's `view` is in device pixels, and `toWorld` multiplies the pointer by `DPR`.** The canvas has been sized in device pixels and every line width scaled by `DPR` since the first commit, while `view` stayed in CSS pixels, so at a ratio of 2 the field filled the top left quarter and the editor's hit test was off by the ratio. Changing the one place `view` is built keeps every draw call as it was; the alternative, scaling the context and stripping `* DPR` from about 30 lines, touches far more for the same result.

**#743. The walker sheet is 1.25 pixels a room unit with no anti-aliasing, 139,488 bytes.** The cap is 160 KB. Two rows, lossless WebP: 1.5 px with 8-sample anti-aliasing 91 KB, 1.5 px FXAA 86 KB, 1.25 px with 8 samples 69 KB, 1.0 px with 8 samples 49 KB, 1.5 px off 48 KB, 1.0 px off 24 KB. Eight rows at 1.35 px off was 159,386 and at 1.25 px off 139,364; the edge pixels' alpha is what the bytes are. The sprites are a little over 1:1 on screen, so the edges step at a device pixel ratio of 2 and the rim pass does not hide it. Lifting the cap to fit anti-aliasing is Devon's call, not this row's (the page would carry about 0.6 MB of images either way). *The site, 2026-09-30.*

**#744. A bone in `walkers.py` is a canvas frame, and every pose is `drawPerson()`'s own number.** A bone is a pivot and the angle its geometry is turned by, measured as `bone()` measures it (from straight down, positive toward +x, angles adding down a chain), so a limb angle is copied across and never re-derived. Each frame sets `pose_bone.matrix`, with a view-layer update after each bone because a child set against a stale parent lands in the wrong place (the first render had every limb scattered). The canvas rotates by `lean` where a bone turns by `-lean`; that sign is the one thing to check if a figure leans backward. Bob and lean are keyed on the body for the twelve walk frames. What the canvas moves with the clock (a cloak's wobble, the ranger's tail, the skirt's sway) moves with the phase, as a swing of its own bone. The standing frame is its own rig with `sw` 0.06, `aSw` 0.12 and the cloak's 0.45 flare, and the page adds the idle bob and lean from the clock as `drawPerson()` does. *The site, 2026-09-30.*

**#745. The tables left the plate, and #732 is closed.** `tavern.py` renders the plate with the tables hidden from the camera but still casting shadow, so their shadow is in the floor, then renders each table as an alpha crop (`table-N.webp`, cut from a plate-sized frame by Blender's border so the pixels are the plate's own) with everything else hidden from the camera and still lighting them. `plateTable()` and its clip are gone; `paintTable()` draws the crop, or the vector table if any of the three failed to load. `room.webp` went from 171,090 to 68,486 bytes, the three crops add 21,352. The border needs a hundredth of a pixel added to each edge: without it Blender floored one of three crops a pixel short, and which one changed with the crop. *The site, 2026-09-30.*

**#746. The page's sprites load all or nothing, and the firelit pass draws a pre-tinted copy of the sheet.** `el.sheet` and `el.tableImgs` stay null until every image has decoded and for good if one fails, so a half-loaded set never draws; the vector figures and tables are the fallback, as the procedural room is (#728). The rim pass cannot take a `fillStyle`, so the sheet is copied once to a canvas and filled with `source-in`, and the pass draws that at the rim's alpha. The names `walkers` and `tables` were already the page's arrays of people and tables; the first wiring overwrote both and threw on every frame. *The site, 2026-09-30.*

**#747. `validate.mjs` holds the sheet cell by cell, and its feet check allows 12 pixels above the anchor.** Every cell has a figure in it, none touches its edge, WALK's cell, anchor, ppu, frames and type order are budget.json's, and TYPE's keys are the rows. The lowest pixel is 222 or 220 in the mid-stride frames, where `drawPerson()`'s bob lifts both feet; a check at 8 pixels failed 14 cells that were right. The row count comes from the budget, not the image, so a missing row reads as empty cells rather than as a smaller sheet. *The site, 2026-09-30.*

**#748. The tavern set's T2 session was launched on Sonnet 5.5, for a row that names Fable 5.1 and runs on Opus 5.5 under #638.** It did the build itself and delegated nothing. Like #734, this records what one session did, not a change to #638. *The site, 2026-09-30.*

**#749. Throneshard's card preview is a teamfight reached by fixed-step play.** The recipe starts a match with the player on autoplay, stubs rendering and steps at `fixedDt = 0.05` until five or more heroes of both teams stand within 10 units of each other with three or more creeps around them and two of the five in an attack or a cast, in daylight (the day phases are 0 to 300 s and 600 to 900 s), then brings rendering back and shoots at camera distance 30 with the hud on. A jungle fight was tried first and the trees hid it, and a night fight read as a black frame. The capture runs under the harness's SwiftShader like the other drafts (#53), so a real-GPU recapture stays an open nicety. *Throneshard, 2026-09-30.*

**#750. A hard bot's last hits were lost to poking and to a greedy threshold, and the fix is a priority, not a difficulty knob.** Over 12 seeds at 10 minutes, hard melee heroes took 10.3 last hits against normal's 15.9. The threshold `lhAcc` at 1.0 and 0.97 attacked creeps that would survive the hit; 0.9 took normal to 17.1 and hard to 14.2. A laner who decided not to fight also rolled its harass chance on every think, so hard, thinking every 0.1 s, poked twice as often as normal; harass now rolls once per half second and a killable creep outranks the poke. Before 11 minutes the engage bar is the same for every difficulty. Hard-versus-hard is still below normal-versus-normal (both sides deny at 0.9 and skirmish), so the fair test is a mixed match: hard's side took 11.5 melee last hits at 10 minutes against normal's 9 in six seeds. The individual hard flags (smart, aware, dive, combo, lead) were each switched off in turn and none explained the gap. *Throneshard, 2026-09-30.*

**#751. A team that is pushing now notices the enemy taking Grimmaw.** The push branch returned before the contest check, so a pushing team never saw it. Noticing without vision now starts at 85% Grimmaw health instead of 70% and rolls 25% a second (50% for aware bots), and a contest needs two living heroes and no more than two fewer than the enemy, where it needed three and one. Contests went from a median of 0 to 1 a match over six seeds (mean 0.3 to 0.8) against 3 to 5 attempts, so most attempts are still not contested: a fight is usually over before a team across the map arrives. *Throneshard, 2026-09-30.*

**#752. The XP table is the old one times 0.42, so level 20 lands near minute 35.** Bot matches ended at 35 to 40 minutes with heroes at level 11 to 15 (mean 13.2 over six seeds), so the talents at 20 and 25 and the scepters almost never came up. With the scaled table the mean is 19.3 at a 33.7-minute median (was 39.6), talents chosen a match went from 13 to 25 and scepters from 1 to 1.5 on average, and the suite's 35.5-minute match had levels 17 to 25. The early levels come faster too: level 2 is 100 XP. Creep bounty XP was left alone. *Throneshard, 2026-09-30.*

**#753. Settings and the game menu are 900 px wide with two and three columns, and the every-second-frame shadow map stays.** At 1280x720 the General tab needed 596 px and Controls & Hotkeys 958 px in a 549 px body; both fit now with no scrollbar (the in-game menu's extra button row was not measured; there is about 100 px to spare). For the shadow map, a hero at 13 units a second was stepped at 1/60 s with the map stale on odd frames and fresh on even ones: the gap is 0.22 units a frame and no frame pair showed a shadow offset. That was a frame sequence on real hardware, not a recording, so a moving-unit look on a slow machine stays worth one real glance. *Throneshard, 2026-09-30.*

**#754. The kill-streak announcer plays recorded CC0 clips, nearest fit, and the formant voice is only a fallback.** The Kenney voice packs have no "Killing Spree" or "Dominating", so the 14 lines map to the closest shouts ("Kill him" for first blood, "Combo" for a double kill, "Combo breaker" for a shutdown) and the banner text does not change. `build_announcer_lines.py` builds them (9 to 17 KB each, 168 KB in all); `speak()` runs only if a clip has not decoded. Sources are in `assets/audio/CREDITS.txt`. *Throneshard, 2026-09-30.*

**#755. Art and effects: Duskward stone is a runtime tint, the melee barracks has a gabled roof, robes are capped, and Rift Wall is still only a picture.** Duskward's stone tint went from `[0.2, 0.14, 0.16]` to `[0.075, 0.068, 0.088]` with no geometry change, and the Sunward pale stone is untouched. The skirt weights follow a thigh by at most 40%, which stops the flap on the robed heroes' run. Bow and rifle attacks are authored in `author_clips.py` (Bow_Shoot 36 frames, Rifle_Shoot on Pistol_Shoot's body), because the Quaternius packs have neither. Ondur's Rift Wall has its stone slabs and crumble but no gameplay wall, so enemies walk through it despite the tooltip; that stays open. The runes lost the generic gold burst for a per-type pickup and aura. *Throneshard, 2026-09-30.*

**#756. The cast is one sheet of rows of different lengths, each row a value the canvas varies and each cell a step of it.** `cast.webp` is 960 by 2944 (6 columns by 16 rows of 160 by 184 cells), 83,152 bytes against the 100 KB cap, at #743's 1.25 px a unit with no anti-aliasing and the walkers' 22 degree turn. A seated patron is 8 rows (four hats by two foods) of 6 drink-cycle steps; the dog is 4 rows (head lift 0, 5, 10, 15 units) of 6 tail angles; the sitting cat is 2 rows (stretch 0 or 1) of 6 tail curls; the walking cat is 6 strides; the barkeep is wipe, pour and idle. The page draws the nearest cell. Steps were kept low because 99 cells cost 83 KB at this size; a third cat stretch and more drink steps were not tried. Each row has its own `ax`, the pixel column its figure's origin stands on, because the dog is 110 units wide and a seated patron 65. *The site, 2026-09-30.*

**#757. The page adds the seated patron's talk lean about the hip, and the barkeep's wipe slide and the pour's stream stay canvas.** The cell has drawSeated()'s rest lean of -0.15; the page rotates the cell by the talk term about the hip, which also moves the bench and shin by up to 2.3 units, where the canvas held them still. The wipe's sideways slide is the page's `translate(kx, 0)` as before, and the amber stream of the pour is still a stroke in the arm's frame, since it is not a silhouette. The rim pass draws the seated patrons from a tinted copy of the sheet exactly as the walkers are (#746); the dog, cat and barkeep were a single black pass in the canvas and stay one. `el.cast` loads on its own, all or nothing, so a failed `cast.webp` leaves the vector cast and keeps the walkers. *The site, 2026-09-30.*

**#758. `walkers.py` runs its sheet from `main()`, and a sheet taller than wide needs `sensor_fit = 'HORIZONTAL'`.** `cast.py` imports `Rig`, `path()`, `key()` and `armature()` from it, which ran the whole render at import; the sheet code moved under a `__main__` guard and the walkers sheet renders byte for byte as before (`cmp` against the committed file). An orthographic camera's `ortho_scale` fits the longer side, so the first cast render came back 3.1 times too large and showed two columns; the walkers sheet is wider than tall and never met it. *The site, 2026-09-30.*

**#759. The five click boxes hold, and nothing on the live page can click them.** With `pointerdown` dispatched on the canvas at 1600 by 900, 1024 by 768 and 390 by 844, a point inside each sprite's body raises the egg it should (dog, fire, cat, door, barkeep) and the boxes are in room units, unchanged. But `#page` (`z-index: 2`, the whole band) sits over `#room` (`z-index: 0`) and `elementFromPoint` over the tavern returns it, so `poke()` gets no real click. It was so before this row. It is a ranked row, not fixed here. The cat also faces -x on the canvas and walks toward +x when `dir` is 1; the sheet copies the drawing and does not fix it. *The site, 2026-09-30.*

**#760. The tavern set's T3 session was launched on Sonnet 5.5, for a row that names Fable 5.1 and runs on Opus 5.5 under #638.** Like #748, it built and delegated nothing, and took T3 alone rather than with rank 2. *The site, 2026-09-30.*

**#761. The pipeline writes only what `gltf.js` reads, and the validator makes that a failing line.** A file is TRIANGLES primitives with POSITION, NORMAL and COLOR_0 (unsigned short normalized), indices, one white material (roughness 0.78, metallic 0, back-face culled), no texcoords and no extension, which is the page's `propMat`. Vertex colours go in a FLOAT_COLOR layer: a byte layer is read as sRGB by the exporter and every colour came out linearized twice (3f6fae read back as 0d296c), which the palette check caught on the first file. *School Generator.*

**#762. Byte stability (#652) took two changes.** `bmesh.ops.create_uvsphere` gives different vertices from one run to the next (chair-task and plant differed in 3 of 3 pairs of runs, found by hashing the mesh inside Blender), so `common.py` writes its own UV sphere, and `recalc_face_normals` is not called because every primitive is wound outward by construction. Three runs of the pack give identical sha256 for all 15 files. Spheres and the pot are flat-shaded, so the pack's leaves are faceted where the page's are smooth. *School Generator.*

**#763. The pack is one file per silhouette, 15 files, and fitting each into its row's box is the wiring row's job.** Four chairs (basic, stack, task, rocker), stool, sofa, desk, table, workstation, counter, labbench, shelf, cubby, locker, plant, each a port of the `js/render.js` builder at one named catalog row, in the page's feet and axes. `budget.json`'s `box` is what the builder draws by hand-count, held to 10%, and each row's w/d/h is checked against `catalog.js`; `left` names what a file omits. The wiring row uses fit `stretch` where a row's dimensions are the intent and `contain` otherwise. *School Generator.*

**#764. The palette is lifted from `render.js`'s tints exactly, and the page's darkest tints come out black.** Computed with the vendored three.js (`Color.setHSL` in its linear working space), leg tint(-0.25) on #3f6fae is 000000, and so are the shelf's sides, the task chair's back and base, the workstation's devices and the lab bench top and handles. The files carry those blacks because the page draws them; a tint in sRGB would keep legs visible. Nothing in `render.js` changed. *School Generator.*

**#765. The batch was launched on Sonnet 5.5, for two rows that name Opus 5.5 and run on Opus 5.5 under #638.** Like #748 and #760, it built and delegated nothing. *School Generator, 2026-09-30.*

**#766. A row names a file only if it is the file's row in colour and builder parameters; 19 of about 50 rows do.** A file carries its source row's colours in its vertices, and `budget.json`'s `left` lists what it leaves out, so `teacher-desk` and `desk-standing` (other browns), `table-art` and `table-prep`, `teacher-chair`, the round, trapezoid, kidney and folding tables, `bench-robotics`, `tote-rack`, `mail-cubbies`, `locker-bank-half`, the printer and sewing tables, the serving and reception counters and the lounge chair, bean bag and cushion keep their builders. `bookshelf-low` and `plant-desk` keep theirs too: the shelf file has one set of compartments, so halving its height squashes them rather than dropping a shelf, and a 0.6 by 0.9 ft plant is not a 1.5 by 4 ft plant scaled. A tint applied at load would admit the other-coloured rows; that is a follow-up, not this row. *School Generator, 2026-09-30.*

**#767. Fit is decided per row: `stretch` for `desk-double`, `stool-lab-30`, `table-seminar-8` and `table-demo`, `contain` for the other 15, and a row is scaled by its size over its source row's.** #763 left the mode to this row. A plain fit into the row's box was wrong for files that draw bigger than their row on purpose (the lab bench's tap makes it 3.58 ft against a 3 ft row; `contain` into 3 ft would have shrunk the whole bench to 5 ft wide), so `builtinBox` takes the file's own size and multiplies each axis by `row / source row`. A source row comes back at exactly the file's scale and the four `stretch` rows are the file's box scaled. `stretch` is asserted to be exactly the rows whose dimensions differ from their source's. The double desk's legs come out about twice as thick as the student desk's, which reads as a heavier desk and was kept. *School Generator, 2026-09-30.*

**#768. The files are fetched after the first draw, for the props a design has, and a failure is an error, not a fallback.** Fifteen requests at boot would have taken it from 109 to 124 against a budget of 115. `main.js` asks for the files the design's props name, after `rebuild()`, swaps the geometry and rebuilds once when they land. The first frame, a recoloured prop (its paint is baked into the vertices, #766's reason again), the walk export (its template carries no file bytes) and a Node test draw the procedural builder, so a walk still shows a school whose furniture is the pre-pack furniture. A file that cannot be fetched or parsed is a `console.error`, which the tool harness's boot check turns into a failure. *School Generator, 2026-09-30.*

**#769. All 15 files are in `PRECACHE`, `REV` is 30.2, and the shell cap in `offline.test.mjs` counts the shell only.** The test capped the precache at 8 entries ("has grown into a manifest"); it now caps the non-model entries at 8 and `test/builtin-models.test.mjs` holds the model entries to the files on disk, both ways. The tool harness's static server mapped every `/assets/` to the repo's; `/assets/models/` is this project's, so it serves those from the project and knows `.glb`. The batch was launched on Sonnet 5.5 for a row that names Opus 5.5 and runs on Opus 5.5 under #638, and delegated nothing. *School Generator, 2026-09-30.*

**#770. Signal City's sheet is wired by `spriteFor` itself: a frame from `cars.json` by `<archetype>/<variant wrapped to 4>/<state>` while px per metre is within [12, 36], otherwise the procedural car.** Rideshare's state is `animFrame`, emergency's `animFrame % 2`. The band is two exported constants in `js/sprites.js` (`SHEET_FROM`, `SHEET_TO`) that `tools/blender/validate.mjs` compares to `budget.json`, so they cannot drift. The frame is drawn once into a canvas of the size the procedural car would be at the target scale, so `render.js`'s `drawImage` is unchanged and a 36 to 12 downscale is not repeated per frame; it is anchored at the frame centre. The trailer goes through `trailerSpriteFor` and `render.js` falls back to `SPRITES.trucker.trailer.draw`. Signal City has no service worker and no precache, so there was nothing to bump; the sheet is two requests (`cars.json`, `cars.png`) fired from `main.js` without awaiting, and a failed read is one `console.error` with the procedural cars carrying on. `setSheet` clears the sprite cache so a car cached procedurally before the sheet landed is replaced. The sim never reads a sprite. Reverse by dropping the `loadSheet()` call in `main.js`. *Signal City.*

**#771. The tavern's click targets are reached by letting pointers through `#page`: `#page { pointer-events: none }` and `#page > * { pointer-events: auto }`, and `#flavor { pointer-events: none }`.** `#flavor` is a caption nothing listens to and its box covered the door at 1600 wide. The hanging sign stays solid: on a 390 wide phone the band is 268 px, the sign takes the top 165 and the fire, door and barkeep sit behind it, a layout fact left alone (the dog is still reachable). Rejected: a document-level pointerdown forwarding to `poke()` (has to guess which targets are interactive), and moving `#room` above `#page` (breaks every link in the band). Guard: `Tools/board-check/check-tavern-clicks.mjs` (`npm run tavern:clicks`, port 8173, a Site CI suite entry) moves a real mouse to each target's screen point, asks `elementFromPoint` who is there, clicks and waits for `tavern-egg`: 24 checks. Broken from green twice: removing the `#page` rule failed 22 (`elementFromPoint` is `div#page`), making `#flavor` solid again failed the door and the dog at 1600 (`p#flavor`). Reverse by deleting those three declarations. *The site.*
**#772. The tavern's sprites are smoothed by crossfading the two cells either side of the value, and the wall detail is canvas, not a second plate.** `drawCast` and `drawSprite` took the nearest of 6 or 12 cells, so a slow wag or a stride moved in whole-cell jumps; both now draw the lower cell at full alpha and the next one over it at the fractional alpha (the firelit pass too). `paintDecor()` adds a banner, a shield, a torch, herb bunches, two pans, barrels, a crate, bar mugs and hearth smoke over the plate, placed in room units around the page's actual view (room x -60 to 1530, y 217 down, the sign hiding x 385 to 1090 above y 445): a first pass at the plate's own coordinates put the banner, antlers and torch entirely behind the sign. Rejected: re-rendering the plate on huginn (31.5 minutes and #733's budget for shapes that sway and flicker), and a vector walk blend (the sheet is the walk). Not guarded: nothing asserts a blend or a decor position; Chromium under SwiftShader (#53) says nothing about frame pacing, so the smoothness is looked at, not measured. Reverse by restoring the two `drawImage` calls and deleting `paintDecor` and its call. *The site.*

**#773. Signal City's sound is `js/audio.js`, every voice synthesized, read off the world's events before the renderer drains them, and capped in wall time: 3 horns and 6 relay clicks a second.** A horn per archetype (the trucker's 110/147 Hz air horn, the aggressive driver's three short blasts), a click when any box changes stage or phase, a siren on the first emergency vehicle on the map whose level and pitch climb from 160 m to 30 m off its box and whose pan is its x across the view, a crash per collision, a chirp per `call` and per `walk`, and a noise bed at 0.12 times the share of 40 cars on the map. The caps are a listener's seconds, not the world's, so 2x does not double the honking. The context is made on the first pointerdown or keydown and never before; `settings.sound`, in the save since version 1 and defaulting to true, is the switch, so `signal_city_v1` and `repair` are untouched. The switch is a header button and the M key: in the panel's foot it wrapped to a second row and pushed Crossing's Rules tab 3 px below the fold at 1280 by 900. Not verified: how any of it sounds (H3). Reverse by dropping the `Sound` lines from `main.js`. *Signal City, R7.*

**#774. Signal City on a phone: the column stretches, the board takes its own touches, two pointers pinch the camera and a touch is a tap within 12 px of travel, reaching 22 px for a car.** Under 760 px `.play` was `align-items: flex-start`, so the board shrank to the canvas's 300 px default on a 390 px screen; `stretch` makes it 366. `touch-action: none` on the board means a drag on it pans and two fingers zoom it, and the page is scrolled from the panel; whether that fights a thumb is H1. The pinch is `zoomBy` by the change in distance between two tracked pointers, and a second finger ends any drag, so nothing a pinch ends in is a tap. A tap reaches `TAP_PX / scale` metres for a car (3.5 m before, 8.7 m at Four Ways' fit), since a fingertip lands off the car. Reverse by reverting `input.js` and the two CSS rules. *Signal City, R8.*

**#775. A queue rule may `then: 'call'`: it latches a call on the phase that serves its movement and cuts nothing; a phase marked `skip` is passed over by `next` until called, and only while the controller has loops to read.** The served phase is the first carrying the movement protected, else the first carrying it at all. A called bought phase has no turn of its own, so it runs after the green it was called in. With every other phase skipped the green rests, and a resting green reads no scheduled end to a driver. Without sensors `skip` means nothing, so a level or a save with no loops cycles as before, and every level that ships without `skip` steps exactly as it did. `after` does not apply to a call. Calls are spent when their phase goes green. Reverse by taking `skip` and the call rules out of a level. *Signal City, R9.*

**#776. Crossing ships actuated: four call rules on its bays, its arrow phases skip, the 24 s elapsed rule unchanged, targets unchanged.** Six seeds with no input: 66 63 61 72 60 70 cleared, wait 37 to 48 s, against 63 59 56 55 61 63 at 47 to 52 s with the `after: 16` queue rules (means 66.0, 42.5 s against 59.5, 50.2 s). One star on every seed either way, no locks, one collision on seed 5. The reference hand beats it still: 73.3, 39.1 s against 66.2, 42.0 s. At 20 s the call form three-stars four seeds with no input, which R2 rules out for a starred level, and 16 s locks one, so 24 stays. Call rules with no `skip` cleared 60.8 at 47.9 s: the skip is what pays. Target 48 and waitTarget 36 hold. *Signal City, R9.*

**#777. A World copies a level's scheduled arrivals before it retries one.** `_spawnTick` retries an arrival whose lane is full a second later by moving its `t`, and `scheduled` was a sliced array of the level's own spawn objects, so the retry moved the level constant: every later World in the same page or process ran the level differently. Free Play is the only level with `spawns`, and it showed: its bare ring on seed 2 cleared 95 as the first run of a process and 92 after any other run, so the calibration tool's six-seed rows were the first seed clean and the rest not. Found by R10's meter sweep, where a meter that never fired changed a run. `test/sim.mjs` schedules two arrivals at once on one lane; with `.slice()` back, the level's second spawn reads 2.53 s and the replay hashes differently. Reverse by nothing: the old behaviour was a bug. *Signal City.*

**#778. Signal City's entry meter is sold on Free Play's ring alone, because it is the one converted board where a meter beats the bare ring on four seeds of six.** The meter (`World.setMeter`, `level.meter`) holds one leg's yield line amber for 2 s and then red for 2 to 12 s whenever a car stands 15 m or more back on the leg that leg's traffic passes first round the ring, then rests green at least 6 s. `tools/calibrate.mjs --ring --meter` swept every leg at 3, 6 and 10 s: First Light's best beats the bare ring's wait on 1 seed of 6, the Stem's on 1, Free Play's S at 3 s on 4 (mean 10.4 to 7.4 s, two seeds tied because it never fired) and S at 6 s on 4 (8.1 s). The first two have balanced legs and wait 1 to 3 s bare, so there is nothing to give back, and they carry `ring.meter: null` and show no meter. Free Play carries `{ leg: 'S', red: 3 }`: the panel offers the meter there, off until a leg is picked, with the red slider starting at 3 s; the leg and the red are the session's and never stored, like the ring's switch (#599), so `signal_city_v1` is unchanged. It comes with the roundabout rather than as a fifth shop row, because it is worth having on one board and that board earns no stars. Not robust: on a test ring with E at 650 an hour, 40% left, the meter on E beat the bare ring on 5 of 6 at 3 and at 5 s and lost seed 2 from 12.2 to 19.7 s at 4 s, so a level that sells one needs its own sweep. `test/stars.mjs` holds the rule for every shipped `ring.meter`. Reverse by setting Free Play's to null. *Signal City, R10.*

**#779. A trucker's trailer is a second body hinged at the tractor's rear, and a turning truck holds only the lanes its tractor or trailer actually cross; a truck turns left deeper than a car.** `Path.trailerHeading` integrates the towed trailer's heading (dθ = sin(φ − θ) ds / wheelbase, wheelbase 7.8 m) once per path, so the trailer cuts inside a turn: 3.2 m on a right, 3.2 m on the truck's left, 0.1 m on a through. `World.sweep` steps the truck every 0.5 m from its front at the box edge until its trailer is out and tests tractor and trailer with `rectsOverlap`, the collisions' own test, against 2.2 m slices of another lane from 8 m behind its stop line to its box exit. `boxVerdict` holds on that in place of `wideConflicts`, which is gone. Against the rule: a right no longer holds the lanes in from its exit leg, a left from the inner of two lanes no longer holds the curb lane beside it, and a left on one lane now also holds the right turn in from its exit leg. Two things the geometry needed that the rule never did. A truck's left is its own path (`Path.truck`, control arms 0.6 against a car's 0.3): on a car's left the trailer, swinging back across the centre line as the truck straightens, reached a car standing 1.5 m behind the stop line of the lane in from its exit leg, which is where that car stands under red while the truck has its green, and that collision locked endless city 5's first day; on the truck's it stops 0.5 m past the line. And a car held by a sweeping truck waits behind the stretch the trailer sweeps, not at the box edge. A truck waiting for a car already standing in its sweep was tried and dropped: it changed none of 30 runs. A right's trailer still rides 2.1 to 2.6 m over a 5 m corner's kerb at any turn shape; nothing stands there. Reverse by restoring the rule in `boxVerdict` and the trailer's `path.at(back)`. *Signal City, R11.*

**#780. Every target holds under the trucker's sweep as geometry; Free Play's ring target moves from 80 to 72 and its meter from S at 3 s to S at 6 s.** Only Four Ways, Two Blocks and Free Play have trucks, and only their tables moved (`calibrate.mjs --baseline --hand`, six seeds). Four Ways: no input locks 6 of 6 as before; the hand clears 72 to 84 at 27 to 42 s, two of six three-starred, no lock, against 70 to 85, 28 to 41 s and one lock; 52 and 32 s hold. Two Blocks: no input 91 to 104 at 11 to 22 s and no three-star, against 85 to 102 at 12 to 19 s; the hand at its best offset, still 16 s, three-stars 5 of 6, against 4; 80 holds and the progression lesson decides the second star. Free Play: no input 70 to 91 at 16 to 29 s against 72 to 91 at 13 to 28 s; 60 and 30 s hold. Free Play's ring clears 74 to 103 at 4 to 15 s, so its target goes under the worst seed as #598 sets them (it was already 78 against 80 after #777); its meter sweep moved too, S at 3 s now beating the bare ring on 2 seeds of 6 and S at 6 s on 4, at a mean of 11.5 s against 10.7, because seed 5 loses 5 s. The rule counts seeds, so S at 6 s ships; #778's warning that the meter is fragile stands. Over 30 runs (the three truck levels and endless days 3 and 5 of six cities, no input) 5 collisions have a truck in them against 3 before, and 24 in all against 17: the new truck ones are trusting starters meeting a trailer still clearing on the all-red, the fault the all-red exists for. Endless city 5 no longer misses day 2 hands-off, which `test/endless.mjs` leaned on to show the hand's commands reach the World; city 16, which misses day 3, carries that line now. *Signal City, R11.*

**#781. A car handed along a corridor draws its turn from every turn the leg allows, and changes lanes on the straight when its lane does not take that turn.** Before, the draw was among the turns its own lane takes, so on two lanes with a left bay a car that went through stayed out of the bay at every box after the first. Now a car whose lane does not take its turn keeps the lane's through and wants the nearest lane that does (`laneWant`), moving one lane at a time when the gap is 2 m plus 0.8 s of each side's speed and of any closing speed (`_laneRoom`), by the zipper's path swap with the body easing across. A truck takes its own left (#779) when it gets there. A car that has not found its gap 25 m before the stop line gives up, keeps its lane and takes the lane's turn (`stats.laneGiveUps`, a `lane-missed` event). On one lane each way every lane takes every turn, so the draw is the old draw: all nine levels on two seeds and two endless days hash as they did, and no calibration moved. No level ships two lanes on a corridor yet; R13's two-lane corridor is the first that will. `test/sim.mjs` holds the move, the truck's left, the give-up and the one-lane case; with the lane's turns only, no gap check or no give-up, a line fails each time. Reverse by drawing from `choices` alone in `_handoff`. *Signal City, R12.*

**#782. Pack 2's first level is Market Ring, a roundabout built as one, whose second star is the entry meter: a lesson, not a wait target, because no wait target splits no input from the meter.** `js/levels/pack-02.js` holds pack 2; `LEVELS` puts it after Main Street and before Free Play, so it opens on Main Street's star. Four legs, one lane, E at 700 an hour with two in five turning left, so its traffic passes N's join, and N at 520; 240 s; target 50, `lesson: { kind: 'meter', wait: 16 }`, met when a meter fired and the average wait came in at 16 s or under, and `ringMeter: { leg: 'E', red: 4 }`. R1's tables, six seeds: no input clears 55 to 65 at 13 to 22 s, locks 2 and three-stars none; the hand, which on a ring level sets the meter the level offers, clears 53 to 69 at 12 to 16 s, three-stars 5 of 6 (seed 4 waits 16.3 s) and locks none. No input's best seed waits 12.8 s, under any wait target the meter could meet on four seeds, so a wait star would be earned by watching (#639). A meter on N or W never fires; on S at 4 s it locks three seeds. A level built as a ring is not `convertible` and takes nothing from the shop, and on it the ring note leaves out the shop's switch. The meter's leg and red are kept per level for the session. `signal_city_v1` is unchanged: a new level id is a new record through `repair` (#36, #37). Reverse by taking the level out of `PACK_02`. *Signal City, R13.*

**#783. Endless keeps the day a run is on as `endless.pending`, so a run closed halfway through a day is taken up again at that day's start; `signal_city_v1` and version 1 are unchanged.** `{ seed, day, days, points, boxes }`, `boxes` one `{ timing, rules }` per box as the day starts (`endless.js boxesOf`, null for a ring), written when a day starts, moved to the next day with that day's rules when a day is survived, and cleared when a run is lost. `repair` reads a save from before it as null, and reads as null anything with no city, no day or no boxes, which loses that run the way every run left halfway was lost before (#36, #37). The select shows "Resume day N" under the Endless card while one is pending; it replays the day from its start with the boxes the day began with (`applyBoxes`), never a half-played day, because nothing mid-day is saved. A new run replaces it. A resumed first day that ends counts as the run's first day, since the run that was left never ended one (#612).
