Reverse by dropping the `pending` writes in `main.js`; the field is then always null. *Signal City, R14.*

**#784. `rectsOverlap` builds nothing per call: its corners go into two scratch arrays, the arithmetic term for term as before.** After R11, `test/endless.mjs` took 1,572 s at three jobs against 848 s on the commit before it, reported by Devon's backup session on huginn. Most of that was not more simulation: endless city 6's day 4 took 24 s alone and 260 s after days 1 to 3 in the same process, with the same 49 cars on the map. The profile put `rectsOverlap`'s per-call closure and corner arrays at 38% where a fresh process spends 7%: once a district's later days had deoptimized them, every probe `obstacleAhead` makes paid for it. Without the allocations, days 1 to 6 of city 6 cost 93 s in one process, as they did before R11, and days 1 to 3 run in under half their old time. Every level on two seeds and two endless days hashes as before, so no answer moved. Declaring `Path._trail` in the constructor was tried first and changed nothing; it stays, because a field added mid-run is the same hazard. Part of the suite's growth is real and stays: R11 let hands-off cities last longer (city 5 went from missing day 2 to lasting six), so there are more days to play. *Signal City.*

**#785. Ranks 14 and 15 ship in one PR as TG-13, and TG-13 is done only when rank 15 is.** Devon's instruction, 2026-10-01: rank 14 (½) and the first increment of rank 15 (2+) went together, against #382's rule that a 2+ row is the whole batch. Both ran on Opus 5.5; rank 15 names Sonnet 5, and the instruction allowed either. Every later increment of rank 15 is TG-13 too. Reverse by splitting rank 15's increments back into batches of their own. *The site, dioramas.*

**#786. The diorama style sheet is `Tools/board-check/blender/dioramas.json`, kept apart from the tavern's `budget.json`, and every render in the set comes from Devon's Windows machine.** A walnut plinth 4.0 by 2.6 by 0.34 m with a brass band and a darker foot; a 70 mm camera 28 degrees up, 24 degrees round and 15 m from the plinth; a warm key, a cool fill and a warm rim, all area lights; a backdrop in screen space from the board's `--wood-light` #2e2114 down to the room's #0d0904, seen only by camera rays; AgX, 256 samples, seed 0, denoised, 2640 by 1600. On the RTX 3070 Ti under OptiX the four cards take 87 s together. The style lives in common.py's diorama section, so a card changes only what stands on the plinth. Reverse by moving the four sections into `budget.json`, which `validate.mjs` would then have to learn to skip. *The site, dioramas.*

**#787. A card stands the game's own `.glb` files on its plinth and builds the rest, and an interior is a cutaway room with two walls.** Golden Hour, Blue Hour, The Fourth Quarter and Aphelion all ship models, so each scene is placements from `dioramas.json` (point, height, turn, scale, repeat), with `patch`, `wall`, `pine`, `window` and `post` built in `diorama.py` for what the game draws in code: sea, path, stream, trees, walls, a star port. Aphelion's diorama is the ship's interior, not its hull on a stand: `hull.glb` rendered as a 3.3 m grey box with nothing to read on it. Reverse a card by editing its entry in `dioramas.json`; nothing else knows about it. *The site, dioramas.*

**#788. `dioramas.mjs` holds every render to one frame, and holds `promote-previews.mjs`'s `KNOWN` to exactly the board's previews.** In CI, with no Blender: 33:20 at 2640 or more, each card a preview `index.html` asks for and never Castle Conundrum (#491), each `.glb` present, each placement on the plinth top, each shape a kind `diorama.py` builds, and `KNOWN` equal to the `data-preview` names (School Generator was missing from it, which is how D1 found it). With `--rendered`, each render's non-backdrop pixels sit inside `frame.safe` (above the og card's crop at 1386 of 1600 rows) and span at least 0.6 of the frame. *The site, dioramas.*

**#789. SkyWings 64's `tools/gpu-profile.mjs` is a measurement, not a check, and stays out of CI.** It exits 0 whatever the frame times are, because no frame budget has been set to fail against, and a hosted runner's GL is software. It exits 1 when the page will not start and when the WebGL renderer string is software, since then every number it prints is wrong. That is #13's rule applied to the one thing it can be wrong about. Reverse by giving it a budget and a non-zero exit once the cuts in the project's WISHLIST item 2 have set one. *SkyWings 64, TG-17 (b).*

**#790. A scene's `statUpdate` fires once, from `afterScene`, and a choice no longer fires its target's.** `handleChoice` used to fire it before the scene as well, so 33 choice-reached scenes paid their deltas twice and showed the stat screen twice. Halving those gains moved no scene path in the nine transcripts. `smoke-save.mjs`'s frozen list of 33 is replaced by a check that no scene carries both a `statUpdate` and choices, and `smoke-page.mjs` fails on a stat screen shown twice for one scene. *Daredevil, TG-08.*

**#791. At Milestone 5, Earl as mentor asks the question before Cal does.** The only road to `earl: 'mentor'` also writes `cal: 'loyal'`, so with Cal's row first `m5_question_earl` was unreachable (#285). Mentor is the narrower state and loyal Cal is most runs, so the narrower row goes first. Ruthie still asks first (#277), and Cal still asks first if he asked in Free Roam 4. Reversible by swapping two rows. *Daredevil, TG-08.*

**#792. A monster Hides and Trips only if its data carries `stealth` or `athletics`, and no shipped adventure's monster does.** Falling back to Perception would hand every monster a skill nobody wrote. With `stealth` it Hides once a turn when at half HP or below, out of reach and with cover, then holds still while hidden from every hero; with `athletics` it opens a turn by Tripping the heaviest armour in reach. Six Harrowmoor Bestiary monsters carry the numbers and the three adventures are untouched, as #97 did with reach. Rock Dwarf's +2 is a `maneuver-dc` conditional bonus now. *Torchbearer, TG-27.*

**#793. A critical Grapple is `grabbed` plus `restrained`, and `restrained` is a list of what is still allowed.** Actions carry no traits, so `RESTRAINED_OK` names the ones with neither attack nor manipulate (End Turn, Delay, Escape, Demoralize, Recall Knowledge, Seek, Hide, Cackle). Spells are off it because nearly all are somatic. #107's Escape DC stands, and one Escape ends both. *Torchbearer, TG-27.*

**#794. Disarm is aimed at the target's first attack, and the condition names it.** A hero picking the weapon would be a second prompt on a one-action maneuver. `disarmPenalty` reads the name per attack in `atkMod` and `strikeMonster`, and a `disarmed` with no `weapon` is −2 on everything, as before. *Torchbearer, TG-27.*

**#795. Ready holds one Strike with any weapon, or one one-action spell aimed at a foe, and is still not a reaction id.** Melee fires on a foe entering reach; ranged and spells fire on a foe entering range and line of sight. Two-action spells are not offered because Ready holds a single action. The pool is spent on firing, and a spent hex or an empty pool does not fire. #109 otherwise stands. *Torchbearer, TG-27.*

**#796. The level-up teaches one spell per slot a rank gains, and stores it in `advances[L].spells`.** In practice that is one rank-2 spell at 4th for every caster, since #114 stops ranks at 2. The field is additive and `repairAdvances` shapes it, so `SAVE_VERSION` stays at 3 (#122's reasoning). A rank with nothing left to learn is satisfied short, as #117 does for feats. *Torchbearer, TG-27.*

**#797. A companion grows with the hero: +1 a level and a share of HP, not a class table.** `companionAt` moves the stat block from its own `level` (default 3) to the hero's: AC, saves, Perception, initiative and attacks +1 a level, HP by `hpPerLevel` (default HP over level + 1), and a striking rune from 4th as `kitAt` gives the hero. Heals and uses do not scale. The five shipped subtitles dropped their baked-in " 3" because the level is printed after them now. *Torchbearer, TG-27.*

**#798. Core feats past 2nd may be honest notes, and four got hooks.** The point was that no class or ancestry slot from 1st to 10th runs dry, so 39 feats went in at 1st to 9th. Each one uses the DSL where it reaches and says "Flavour only" in its own text where it does not. `blind-fight`, `gang-up`, `kip-up` and `mountains-stoutness` are new wired hooks (guide §8 is at 49). *Torchbearer, TG-27.*

**#799. Absalom's sheets are lit by the surface normal, not by lamps: emission of `PALETTE`'s top, left and right colours weighted by the normal's +Z, +X and +Y squared, then a short ambient occlusion.** The plan asked for lights set so a block's faces come out at the palette's values. Lamps can only approach that, and this gets it exactly: a face square to an axis is its colour to within 1 level (`validate.mjs` samples every named face's centre and allows 4), a bevel or a curve blends between them, and a face turned from all three falls toward 0.45 of `left`. Cycles on the CPU, 64 samples, seed 0, no denoiser, byte-identical on a rerun on huginn. Reverse by giving `face_mat` a Principled BSDF and a sun. *Absalom, TG-09.*

**#800. A thing `PALETTE` gives one colour (the stairs, the gold, a floor shade) takes its side faces at the wall's ratios, 0.61 and 0.76 of the top in sRGB, rather than from new hexes.** The plan says a new colour is a decision; this avoids eleven of them by reading the ratio `wallLeft`/`wallTop` and `wallRight`/`wallTop` already set (0.60 to 0.63 and 0.75 to 0.78 by channel). Reverse by adding the side colours to `PALETTE`. *Absalom, TG-09.*

**#801. The heir is one frame rendered as a mask, red, green and blue for the build's top, left and right, and `render.js`'s `tint()` mixes the palette in linear light once per palette.** An heir's colours are content, so a render per build would make every new pack a Blender job. Mixing in linear light is how the renderer mixed the fixed-colour figures, so a tinted heir is shaded the way a rendered one would be, and a pure red pixel comes out exactly `palette.top`. `validate.mjs` fails a mask where a channel never leads, and `browser.mjs` counts the fighter's three faces on the board and none of the wizard's; with the wizard's palette hard-coded both lines fail. `palette` stays three colours, so no pack changes. *Absalom, TG-09.*

**#802. The sheets load before the board is built, and a sheet that will not load stops the boot with the file named in the hint bar; there is no procedural fallback.** The prisms are gone from `render.js`, as the common plan's wiring rule asks (a suite line, not a silent fallback). `main.js` awaits `loadSprites()` after the pack and before the picker's result is built on, the same place an unreadable pack stops it. `browser.mjs` blocks `tiles.png` and expects the message; with the decode error swallowed, the hint line and the file-name line fail. Reverse by catching in `main.js` and keeping a copy of `prism()`. *Absalom, TG-09.*

**#803. Faire Weekend's marker sheet is line work, not lighting: every surface a flat emission of one plat colour, and Freestyle drawing the edges in `INK`.** The plan asked for "line work rather than shading" in the plat's ink. Emission at strength 1 under the Standard view transform returns a palette hex as that hex, and Freestyle (silhouettes, creases under 150 degrees, material edges, 3 px) is Blender's own pen, so a plank or an awning stripe gets its line by being a second material of the same colour. Cycles on the CPU, 32 samples, seed 0, no denoiser: byte-identical on a rerun on huginn; Workbench was not an option because it draws no Freestyle. `plat.js` exports `PAPER`, the compass's `#F2E6C6`, rather than the sheet inventing a canvas colour. A frame is footprint x 48 px x 2 (48 is the largest `--cell`), and the front gate is a frame like a 1 x 1 plot. Reverse by giving `flat()` a Principled BSDF and a light. *Faire Weekend, TG-10.*

**#804. `ui.js` imports the marker atlas as a JSON module, the drawing is an `aria-hidden` span with a percent background, and the glyph moves to the front of the title, which is also the `role="img"` marker's `aria-label`.** Importing makes a missing atlas stop the page (the common plan's fail-loud wiring) while `render` stays a pure function of state; percent sizes let one frame fill a 42 px plot, a 93 px stage or the gate's cell without the page knowing a pixel size. Node 22 prints an ExperimentalWarning for the import; the suites pass under it. Ghost, blocked and moving markers keep their classes and their `+`, `✕` and arrow. Reverse by fetching the atlas in `main.js` before the first render. *Faire Weekend, TG-10.*

**#805. Hearth's building sheet is drawn in the map's own projection, one frame pixel to one map pixel, each pixel sampled at its centre, and shaded by which way a face is turned.** The map draws a building's ground where it stands and its height straight up the screen, so `common.py` puts every vertex through (x, y - z) with depth y + z and looks straight down; a south face is the elevation the game drew, which keeps every door, window and lamp on the pixel it was on. Cycles with one sample through its 0.01 px pixel filter makes every pixel one flat colour or clear, which is how the game's own `fillRect`s look blown up with smoothing off, and a frame's sides and anchor are whole tiles so the game never resamples it. A face takes its palette hex times a tier (south 1.0, top 1.12, west 0.88, east 0.72, north 0.6, in `budget.json`); the tiers are new colours, derived the way `drawFace`'s `shade()` derives one, and the validator allows render.js's colours at those tiers and nothing else. Reverse by giving the camera a tilt and dropping `project()`. *Hearth, TG-11.*

**#806. The atlas is `assets/sprites/buildings.js`, one JSON literal on `const BSHEET=`, loaded by `<script>` before `render.js`; the loop waits for the picture, and a sheet that will not load stops the island with the reason in the log.** Hearth's scripts are classic and the game still runs from `file://` (the harness opens it that way), where a `fetch` of a JSON file is refused, so "loads as data, not as an import" is a data-only script rather than a request. `sheetGo()` starts the loop on the image's load; a failure says it in the log and prints `hearth: <why>`, which every harness mode already counts as a failure, and `openIsland` waits for the sheet so every mode fails by name without one. A frame missing from the atlas throws in `draw()`. Reverse by fetching the atlas once the harness serves the page over http. *Hearth, TG-11.*

**#807. What moves or changes stays drawn over the frames: the mill's sails, a lit window, the lighthouse lamp, the kiln's chimney pots, the hall's shelf, the hut's boat and a building going up; snow is a rendered frame per kind drawn at the snow's depth.** Turning sails would be resampled as a rotated sprite, and the rest depend on the hour, a way learnt or the hall's shelf. Snow used to grow as a triangle from the ridge; it is now every upward face of the model (normal at least 0.3 up) in `#eef2f4`, the rest held out, drawn at `globalAlpha = cap`, for the five kinds the game snowed on before. A house's roof is one of the three `sim.js` picks; a roof from anywhere else (a hand-made link) takes the nearest frame rather than none. The faces are untouched (#646). Reverse by putting the old snow triangles back over the frames. *Hearth, TG-11.*
**#808. Corner & Kettle's cup is a glass seen from 30 degrees above the counter, drawn as stacked frames in stepped flat shading with the old stroke's ink; the customer stays the 10 x 14 pixel pattern.** A glass because the old SVG's white cup showed its liquid through its side, and the drink's colour is what the player is building; it is cut into the half behind the drink and the half in front so the liquid, ice and toppings sit between them. Every frame is the old 100 x 130 viewBox at 2 px a unit, so layers line up by being drawn at one place, and the body lands at 21,33 to 92,125 against the old 22,34 to 92,124. Shading is an emission stepped in four bands from 0.76 to 1 by the normal against one fixed light: smooth gradients put the sheet at 180 KB and read against the shop's flat UI. The customer stays pixel art because a 10 x 14 grid recoloured per customer from five palette slots is what gives a queue of strangers its variety, and a rendered figure would need a frame per hair, skin, shirt and pants combination or a tint mask per slot for a 60 px sprite. Reverse by restoring `cupSvg` from `1bd6a60^`. *Corner & Kettle, TG-12.*
**#809. The colours the old SVG did not have are `budget.json`'s `colours`: the glass is white at 0.42 behind and 0.07 in front, and the four foods take new browns.** Ice, foam, whip, cinnamon, the sprinkles and the straw keep the old SVG's fills; the two drizzles are one grey frame tinted to the old strokes, `#c98a3a` and `#4a2a1c`. The foods had no drawing, only an emoji, so their colours (croissant `#e0a650`/`#c98a3a`, bagel `#c98a4a` with `#f3e6c4` seeds, muffin `#a8692f` in a `#e8d9a8`/`#d4c08a` wrapper, cookie `#d6a15c` with `#4a2a1c` chips) are new. The front glass started at 0.16 and washed a drip coffee to grey through its two faces, so it went to 0.07. Reverse by editing `colours` and rerunning `cups.py`. *Corner & Kettle, TG-12.*
**#810. The bubble draws the recipe's icon and the cup the order asks for, a food order and a station's plate draw the food's frame, and a sheet that will not load is a toast and a console error while the shop keeps running.** The old bubble's milk, syrup, topping and ice emoji said that something was wanted, not what; `orderCup(order)` builds the wanted cup from the recipe's base and the order's milk, syrup, toppings and ice, and the bubble draws it 22 px tall with no steam. The recipe's emoji stays first because two recipes can ask for the same cup (#365) and the name is how a player tells them apart at a glance. A missing sheet does not stop the shop the way Hearth's does (#806), because every ticket is also words and the game is playable blind to the pictures; `ui.js` loads the picture itself, since an `<image>` that fails says nothing. Reverse by putting the emoji back in `orderIconsHtml`. *Corner & Kettle, TG-12.*

**#811. The black hole's and the wormhole's cores are flat in the sheet, and the wormhole's glow goes behind its frame with the black hole's.** Workbench's studio highlight ignores `roughness` and `specular_intensity`; a material at full metallic takes the highlight in its base colour, so a near-black core stays near black (`material(..., flat=True)`, set on `hole_core` and `worm_core` only; the global highlight stays on for the star). Centre pixels went from 69,70,75 and 65,73,75 to 6,2,19 and 6,36,33 against the game's old 5,1,15 and 4,33,29; the small lift is Workbench's ambient. With a dark core the wormhole's glow no longer turns it teal, so it joins the black hole under its frame and #736's exception for the wormhole is closed. Reverse by dropping `flat=True` and putting `under` back to the black hole alone. *Orbital, TG-19.*

**#812. In Hearth, a grown adult who remembers the dead may tell a child about them when no elder can, and an elder still goes first.** Remembers: related to them, or on the island and five or older the day the stone went up, the same five the child is measured against. Seed 7 has somebody past sixty on 57 days in 700, so the only durable record a generation leaves fired about once in thirty-five game-years. On Chromium 1194 over 700 days, children told went from 5 and 1 to 13 and 12 on seeds 7 and 20260819, and the days it could happen from 138 and 54 to 499 and 575. The adult pass runs only when the elder pass finds nobody, and on the same draws, so the forty-day pinned hashes did not move for it. *Hearth, TG-26.*

**#813. A house going up is a plot in `plots`, saved as `hp` in save v17, and the next free adult with nobody on it picks it back up.** It used to live only on its builder's `p.tgt`, so nightfall, a storm or a save dropped the plot and its 18 wood: on `main`, seed 20260819 paced 21 plots in forty days and finished 15, seed 42 lost 3. The pickup draws no `R()` and `freeSpot` keeps off a plot. The fix moved both pinned hashes on Chromium 1194, 41eb3cb5:37275 to 57b8fcbc:36695 on seed 7 and 4ac7d23d:36964 to 7a21f4f3:41312 on 20260819; reverse by deleting the hop and the two branches. *Hearth, TG-26.*

**#814. Nobody sails off a Hearth island in its first ten days (`FIRST_DAYS`).** Thunder held from day 1 to day 10 took 12 people off 6 islands of 10 by day 40 on `main`; with the guard nobody leaves before day 11 and 3 leave later, from 3 islands. Natural weather never gets close: 30 random islands put at most 6% of their first twenty days in thunder and lost nobody by day 40, so this is the worst case only, and the test sits before the draw so no other island's stream moves. *Hearth, TG-26.*

**#815. Hearth's chronicle stays whole; what is bounded is its cost, and `debt` measures it.** `events` (40) and every `hist` (60) were already capped, and the standing backlog's line saying otherwise was stale. `chron` is the record itself, and songs, ways and named places index into it, so trimming it would lose the thing the island is for. Seed 7 to day 701: 538 entries in 85,754 bytes (122 a day), a 205,364-byte save, `draw()` 2.7 ms median and a day's steps flat at about 400 ms wall from day 120 on. `debt` fails past 600 save bytes or 200 chronicle bytes a day after day 400, or a 40 ms median draw. *Hearth, TG-26.*

**#816. Hearth has a Node unit suite: `test/load.mjs` runs the real scripts in a vm context with a stub page, and `test/unit.mjs` calls into them.** No browser and no install, so it runs first in the gate. Node's V8 is not Chromium 1194's, so nothing there compares against `hashes.json`; it compares the code with itself. Extracting real modules stays in the later arc. *Hearth, TG-26.*

**#817. Hearth's board preview is captured by its own headless script, `Tools/board-check/capture-hearth.mjs`, not by a `games.mjs` entry.** #84 kept Hearth out of `games.mjs` because the board's suite is headed and would copy the harness's `save` mode; the picture is the only part the board is missing, and a 2D canvas needs no screen, so one file seeds island 7, steps it to late morning of day 30 in clear weather, zooms on the village and exits 1 if the building sheet failed, the island is empty or the page threw. It hides the controls hint, which crosses the bottom of a 330 px thumbnail, and nothing else.
**#818. A prop's tint is HSL in sRGB, and it is one function: `tintSrgb` in `js/builtin-models.js`, which `render.js`'s `tint` calls.** #764 found `render.js` tinting in three.js's linear working space, where the student chair's blue has a lightness of 0.24, so its legs at -0.25 were 000000, and so were 13 more of the pack's 46 roles (the shelf's sides, the table's legs). In sRGB the same leg is 1d3350. Every procedural prop on the page moves with it, not only the pack: dark tints come out lighter and light tints a little less light (the stool seat 6d7682 to 535c66). Four roles are still 000000 or 030304 because their row is nearly black to begin with (the lab bench top, the task chair's casters and legs, the stool's legs). The 15 files were rebuilt on huginn from the new palette, twice, byte-identical. `validate.mjs` (554 to 630 assertions) now fails a palette entry that is not its recipe applied to the row's colour, so `budget.json`'s hex cannot be edited by hand. `REV` is 30.3. Reverse by restoring `tint`'s two `getHSL`/`setHSL` lines and rebuilding the pack. *School Generator, 2026-10-03.*

**#819. A file is repainted by role as it loads, so colour no longer decides which rows name one: 24 rows do, and a recoloured prop keeps the file's shape.** `BUILTIN_MODELS[id].tints` says how the builder makes each colour in a file from the row's one `color` (`leg: [[-0.25]]`, the lab bench's door two steps, its tap a fixed `#b8bcc2`). `retint` matches each vertex to a role by its colour, to one 8-bit step, and paints it that role of the new colour; a fixed colour stays, so a blue plant keeps a terracotta pot. `teacher-desk`, `desk-standing`, `table-art`, `table-prep` and `teacher-chair` join #766's 19, all `stretch`, and #768's "a recoloured prop draws the procedural builder" is gone. Matching by colour needs two roles made differently never to share one, and the validator fails a file where two are within two steps. Rejected: recovering each vertex's lightness delta from the file with no table, which loses a tint that clamped and has to guess which greys are the row's. The rows #766 kept out for shape stay out. *School Generator, 2026-10-03.*

**#820. A walk export carries the pack's files its props name, in a third slot, and the walk has them before its first frame.** `<!--SG-MODELS-->` beside the design and the bake: a JSON object of base64 by id, through `share.js`'s codec. The sample school's four files are 12.8 KB of a 3.1 MB walk. `walk-main.js` hands them to the renderer before `buildFromState`, so no frame draws a stand-in. An export with the marker still standing, which is every walk made before this, draws the procedural furniture as it did. The button fetches the files again and stops the export if one cannot be fetched, #768's rule; a slot that will not decode on the far side is a `console.error` and a walk on procedural furniture, since a walk has no network to ask. `test/export-walk.test.mjs` now pins `main.js`'s three markers to the bundler's, which its comments claimed and nothing did. *School Generator, 2026-10-03.*

**#821. The live site gzips `.gltf` and `.glb`, and its responses are GitHub Pages': Cloudflare holds the DNS, not the proxy.** `curl -sI -H 'Accept-Encoding: gzip, br'` on 2026-10-03 returned `content-encoding: gzip` and `server: GitHub.com` for `Projects/throneshard/assets/models/Horse.glb` (181,808 bytes raw, content-length 68,939) and for `Projects/bell-to-bell/Assets/models/blender/window.gltf` (2,508 raw, 774 sent), with no `cf-ray` header; the A records are GitHub's 185.199.108 to .111.153 and the name servers are Cloudflare's. That settles the question #619 and #704 left open: the host compresses, so raw-under-gzipped (#619) is stricter than needed and stays as the safe rule, and #704's slack for a host that does not gzip is no longer a live case. Devon's note of 2026-10-03 says the site is served through Cloudflare; the measurement says Cloudflare is the DNS and nothing is proxied yet, so re-measure if that changes. *The site, 2026-10-03.*

**#822. School Generator's daylight factor is the BRE average, its orientation is sun hours, and both are notes: the code's line stays the glazing ratio.** The standing backlog said "a glazing ratio, not a daylight factor, nothing knows about orientation, overhangs or room depth". `daylight.js` now gives each room `adf` = T·ΣAw·θ / (A·(1−R²)) with T 0.612 and R 0.5, where θ is the sky each pane sees between the top of whatever wing stands opposite and the underside of a storey jettied out above or a pitched roof's 1.5 ft eave; `deep`, the limiting depth L/W + L/H ≤ 4, judged only when all the glass is within 30° of one wall; and `sunHours`, the hours between 08:00 and 16:00 on the design's own date, latitude and north that direct sun stands in that band. An overcast sky has no compass, so turning the building moves `sunHours` and leaves `adf` exactly equal, and a test says so. A room is `dim` only once it has passed the ratio, so a room short of glass has one finding and not two. `daylight-factor` and `deep-room` are level `note` and cite BS 8206-2, not the IBC: a generated 2,400-student school has 85 to 103 dim rooms and 120 to 134 deep ones, which as warnings would bury the egress findings. The building is its own only obstruction. Cost: 132 to 161 ms on those schools, against a report of 3.6 to 8.3 s. To reverse: delete `readSky` and the two findings; nothing is saved, so no storage key or version moved. *School Generator, 2026-10-03.*

**#823. School Generator paints a wall by the face: each long face takes the paint of the room in front of it, the run is cut where that room changes, and nothing new is saved.** The standing backlog said "one colour per wall, not per face": a red room beside a blue one shared a wall that was one of the two on both sides (the lower hex), and a painted room painted the plain corridor's side of its wall. `finish.js`'s `facePainter` reads a run as stretches `{ t0, t1, left, right, body }` from where a line 0.1 ft off the run crosses a room's outline, not from samples, so a wall with two rooms behind it changes colour on the partition. The probe is 0.1 ft and not `wallPaint`'s 1.2 because rooms meet on a wall's centreline, and at a 45 degree corner a 1.2 ft probe is outside the room for the first 1.2 ft of the wall. A stretch under 1 ft goes to its longer neighbour. `left` is null where there is no room, and the facade still goes on last. The top and the ends keep `wallPaint`'s rule, asked per stretch. `render.js` already wrote one face of a wall box for the facade (Phase 5), so this is two more writes per box and no second mesh. Derived from `shape.paint`, so no save change; an accent wall would need a colour stored per ring segment and is not this. Reverse it by handing `addOriented` a skin with `out` only.

**#824. A switchback ramp is a serpentine: each run takes a lane of its own beside the last, 30in of rise a run at most, a 5ft level landing at every turn and at the top.** ADA 405.6 caps one run's rise at 30in and 405.7 wants a 60in landing wherever it stops or turns, so a 12ft storey at 1:12 is five runs of 28.8ft, and `switchback.js` lays them side by side in 20ft by 38.8ft against 144ft straight. Side by side and not stacked like a dog-leg stair, because a stacked ramp is over itself: the surface would have two heights at one point in plan, which `stairSurfaceAt`, the collider and the navmesh all assume it never has, and the hole in the floor above would stop being boxes. The cost is width. The module is geometry only (layout, surface height, the cut, the guards) on plain numbers, with a run count that is built as asked and marked `legal: false` when it breaks the 30in rule, the way a steep ramp is built and reported. No link stores a run count and nothing in the page calls it: wiring a save field and a dozen consumers in one go was more than one session could verify. Reverse it by deleting the two files.

**#825. A folded ramp is two optional numbers on the ramp link, `data.runs` and `data.side`, clamped where they are read, and the save version stays 12.** A link's `data` is already a bag of scalars that `width` and `slope` are read out of through a clamping accessor, so `rampRuns` and `rampSide` in `stairs.js` do the same and a link without them is one run: every ramp saved before this reads as the ramp it was, and `addStair` writes neither field for a straight ramp, so a straight ramp's record is field for field what it always was. No `migrate` step, because nothing drifted; no `repair` step, because a nonsense count reads as a number in range. The version does not move for the reason a phasing plan and a rate table did not move it: an older build opens the file and draws a straight ramp, which is wrong about the ramp and loses nothing. `stairs.js` imports `switchback.js`, not the other way, so the seven ramp constants are defined in `switchback.js` and re-exported: the walk export's bundler refuses an import cycle. The hole in the floor above is one ring (a row of per-lane columns), not several boxes, because two holes sharing an edge do not triangulate. The guards on the ramp are colliders on the storey it climbs from, at a 0.1ft pad and not a wall's 0.25, or a 4ft lane has 1.7ft of play.

**#826. A folded ramp's guards that stand at the upper floor's height are colliders on the upper storey too, and they are picked by their height, not by name.** `storeyAt` puts somebody on the top landing on the storey above, where #825's guards (lower storey only) are not in the collider, and the side of the landing that faces the next lane has no rail of the hole's, because the landing beside it is inside the hole as well. `rampTopGuardSegments` in `stairs.js` returns every `rampGuards` piece with both ends at the rise, which is the two sides of the top landing and nothing else, and `rampGuardSegs` hands them to the storey the link climbs to at the same 0.1ft pad. Nothing new is drawn: these are guards `render.js` already draws, which is why they did not go into `openingRails`, where `render.js` would draw them a second time. Nothing is saved.

**#827. An accent wall is an optional `accents` array on a room's ring, beside `walls`, read with a default, and the save version stays 12.** `ring.accents[i]` is the colour of that room's own face of segment i, or null for the room's paint, and the key exists only while some segment has a colour: a ring nobody has accented has no key in memory or in a file, so every design saved before this reads as it did and is written back as the same bytes. No `migrate` step, because nothing drifted; no `repair` step, because `readRing` turns anything that is not a `#rrggbb` into null and an array of nulls into no array. A ring that loses a point in `cleanRing` on the way in has renumbered its segments, so its accents are dropped, not moved to another wall. The version does not move for #825's reason: an older build opens the file, paints the wall the room's colour and loses nothing but the accent on its next save. It is stored on the ring and not as a per-wall record because the room is on a ring's left whichever ring it is, so a segment index names one face without a side field, and because `shapes.js` already keeps `walls` and `openings` on their segments through every edit. An accent does not need a wall on its own segment: the bake keeps a shared wall on one room's ring and leaves `SEG_NONE` on the other's, and the second room still has a face of it. `facePainter` matches an accent to a run by geometry (both ends within 0.01ft of the run's line), not by index, because `render.js` asks about runs, not segments. The top and the ends of a wall keep the rooms' own paint. Nothing sets one from the page yet: the control is chrome and waits for a `test/visual` baseline.

**#828. School Generator's tool suite draws the page at half scale: `deviceScaleFactor: 0.5` on the same 1600 by 950 viewport.** The software rasterizer is fill-bound (970ms a frame at 1600x950, 640ms at 1280x760, 290ms at 800x475, one scene), every Playwright round trip waits for one frame, and no check in `test/tools/run.mjs` reads a pixel. The layout is in CSS pixels, so no gesture and no panel moves. `--headed` keeps 1. `test/visual` is untouched and stays at its own scale.

**#829. The Conversion Codex's two numbers on a bound are outliers held by a clamp, and no table changes for them.** The medusa's AC 20 against Paizo's 25: PF1e prints AC 15 at CR 7, five under Table 1-1's 20, and Paizo rebuilt her on high AC, so the source is the outlier; 20 is the lowest AC the converter writes at level 7, and no AC anchor from 1.5 to 3 moves it. The nalfeshnee's Will 31 against 23: PF1e's +21 is four over CR 14's good save, and Paizo printed Will a third of the way from low to moderate, so the PF2e side is the outlier; 31 is `bench`'s 4.5 cap, and no save anchor from 2.5 up moves it. The four rows the two read (Table 1-1 at CR 7 and 14, GM Core's AC at 7 and saves at 14) were read against the printed tables from memory, not refetched, and agree. Two rules were measured and not taken, because each is a choice of Devon's: capping saves at extreme moves six saves toward print and none away (2.12 to 2.07 mean error, the nalfeshnee to +7), and flooring AC at low takes the medusa to -3 and the lich to -1 but the gelatinous cube from +4 to +6. `converter-convert.test.mjs` names the eleven numbers on a bound or one short of it. *Pathfinder Converter, 2026-10-03.*

**#830. Absalom's `settled` column was right and its driver could not fill it; a second driver that breaks line of sight fills it, and the engine is untouched.** `checkDisengage()` calls `endCombat("lost")`, the `mode` event carries the `why`, the watcher closes that fight as `settled` and `encounterRows` counts it: every link was correct and none had been run. Over `balance.mjs`'s 8,000 seeded runs `combatPolicy` ended 43,413 turns in an encounter and none out of sight of an awake construct. `makeSkulkPolicy` steps out of sight of each construct once, then fights it; `balance.mjs` plays 100 runs of it per build and exits non-zero if no fight settles. The main batch still reads 0.0% and says why. The heal, the monsters and the tuning are Devon's and did not move; `baseline.json` is unchanged.

**#831. The Conversion Codex stops a save at extreme: `bench` caps a save's tier at 4, and every other stat still at 4.5.** Devon took the first of #829's two measured rules on 2026-10-04. The highest save the converter writes is now the one the GM Core prints in the extreme column for the level. Re-measured on the 48 pairs before and after: six saves move, all toward Paizo's number and none away (gelatinous cube Fort 15 to 14 against 12, owlbear Fort 16 to 15 against 13, troll Fort 18 to 17 against 17, balor Fort 41 to 39 against 39, nalfeshnee Fort 31 to 30 against 28 and Will 31 to 30 against 23), mean save error 2.12 to 2.07, and no other field of any pair changes. The AC floor is not approved and AC is untouched. *Pathfinder Converter, 2026-10-04.*

**#832. A ramp's fold and an accent wall are set from the tool panels, and neither moves a `test/visual` baseline.** The Stairs panel has a Runs stepper and a side switch, shown while a ramp is what the tool places or what is selected: with a ramp selected they re-fold it through `setRampFold`, one undo step each and only the field pressed; otherwise they set the next ramp placed, and the two are kept apart. Nothing refuses a count that is too few, because a design mid-edit may be wrong and #833 says so. The Wall panel has nine Accent wall swatches: one arms a brush, a click paints the face of the nearest wall of the room the cursor is in (`accentFaceAt`, 3ft of reach), the same colour again takes it off, the dashed swatch clears, and Esc or a wall type puts the brush down. The face is picked by the room under the cursor and not by `nearestSegment`, because two rooms sharing a wall each have a segment on its line and the first found would take the paint from either side. #827 and the standing backlog said these controls would move `chrome-rail`; they do not. Every tool panel is a `tool-slot` hidden unless its tool is picked, the captures are taken with the Floor tool, and the seven candidates huginn drew before and after are byte for byte the same.

**#833. A ramp run that rises more than 30in is a `ramp-rise` warning, and the ramp still counts as a way up.** ADA 2010 405.6. `rampOverRise` in `stairs.js` asks it of every ramp, with the tolerance `switchbackLayout`'s `legal` uses, so a 10ft storey in four runs is on the line and passes. The finding names the worst run in inches and the fewest runs that cure it, or says a lift is the answer when twelve runs are still over. It is a warning and not a fail, and the ramp stays on the accessible route, because the slope is legal and the chair in the walkthrough climbs it; taking it off the route would turn every straight floor-to-floor ramp into a `no-lift` fail on designs that had none. That is the cheap half to reverse: one filter in `accessibleAnalysis`. The sample school has a stair, a lift and an opening and no ramp, so its report does not gain the finding and `chrome-rail` does not move, which the backlog had the other way.

**#834. A one-letter typo the book prints once against its own spelling everywhere else is corrected, and the page is the record.** `Lighting` for Lightning (rules p96 Monsoon, p97 Steelforge, against the other six alignments and every other table) and `Mecurian` for Mercurian (campaign book p169's timeline row, against eight `Mercurian` on p85 and p87). Neither is a rule, a number or a missing sentence, so nothing is invented, and a test pins each. Reversed by putting the letter back in `timeline.json` and the hidden table and re-running the extractor.

**#835. A defect the book prints in a cell the PDF itself shows is left as printed, and the item is reworded to name the page and the exact words.** `First Aid` and `Diagnose` (p48), `Healing Venom` (p73), `Shift Loads` (p71), `Take Ground` (p70), `Hand out Weapons` and `Reverse Protection` (p69), the three Savant blanks (p80) and `Empower Fire`/`Enhance Fire` `N/A` (p58) are all in the rendered page, not lost by the extractor. Picking 2 or 3, or writing the missing half-sentence, is the rules team's call. They stay in `Numina/WISHLIST.md` as "confirmed in the book".

**#836. Numina's social card is drawn with `test/a11y`'s Playwright on a server of its own, and `npm test` holds a hash of what it was drawn from.** `npm run card` resolves `playwright` from `Numina/test/a11y` with `createRequire`, so the site's own `package.json` still installs no browser, and serves the repo root on port 0 instead of expecting somebody's `http-server` on 8099. `tools/social-card-inputs.mjs` hashes the first `:root` block of `src/css/main.css` and `world-map.njk`; the tool writes that hash to `tools/social-card.inputs.json` when it draws the committed card, and `smoke.mjs` fails when the files no longer match. It does not see a `.map-region` rule, a font file or the card's layout. The committed PNG was not redrawn: the pinned Chromium (1194) reproduced every colour and shape and moved 23,609 glyph-edge pixels of 756,000, which is a browser difference and not a palette one.

**#837. Every name on Notable Figures whose entry points at a page has to be on that page, and a page that never named the person is a listed gap, not a pass.** `smoke.mjs` reads the bold runs and the tables' first columns of `notable-figures.md`; an entry points at the pages it links, its section's "See" line, and for a table the paragraph that introduces it. A leading title comes off (`Empress`, `King`, `The`) and the rest must be there whole: dropping words until something matched let `Eviar Leonate` pass on `Sawyer Leonate`, and the break that showed it was renaming Eviar on the built Ophrailes page. 125 names are checked with a floor of 120; 63 point nowhere and are counted, not checked. `Stovaresk` pointed at The Realms and is on Rues, so the entry links Rues. `Reach Lord Sarah Jackson` links the Reach, which names neither her nor Hadley's Cove, and nothing else in the repo does: she is the one entry in `KNOWN`, which fails the day she is on the page.

**#838. `world.md` stays at 397 words, because that is the book's chapter.** The campaign book's Introduction paragraph and p5 to p6 are on the page in full. Anything longer is new writing, which the site does not do, or a digest of the nation pages, which the home page and `lore/index` already are. The WISHLIST item says so.

**#839. Numina's builder offers Excellencies as a list, and a build still holds them by name.** Step 5 is the chapter's 30 and the hidden table's 18, the same 48 for every Domain, because `excellencies.md` says "You may take any Excellency regardless of your chosen Domain". The box's value is the name, so the `x=` fragment and the `numina.build` save are the shape #306 wrote: a typed name ticks its box in any case, and a name on neither list gets a box of its own and prices by its tier as before. The skills inside an Excellency are still not priced, and the step says so.

**#840. The same Excellency twice is one Excellency.** `priceBuild` reports `duplicate-selection` at step 5 and charges it once, as it already does for an Aspect or an Expression. The book does not say it in words; a typed-name save holding `Deadeye` and `deadeye` would otherwise tick one box and be charged 11 CP. Reverse it by deleting the check in `build-rules.js`.

**#841. A skill inside an Excellency costs its printed CP on top of the Excellency, and its `Included` row comes with it.** The rulebook's "Reading Skill Listings" (p41) says "Cost: The number of CP (Character Points) required to purchase the skill", and step 7 says skills "come from all the different things you have selected previously". So a build has a list, `excellencySkills` (fragment key `xs`, storage key unchanged, #36), step 7 sells the 209 priced rows of the tables the build holds, and `priceBuild` grants the row whose cost is `Included` (Slayer's is its fourth, so by `cost.kind`, not position) and reports `excellency-skill-group` for a skill from an Excellency the build does not hold. The 18 hidden Excellencies and a typed name have no table in the book: nothing inside them is listed or priced, and steps 5 and 7 say so.

**#842. A prerequisite stated only in one row's description is printed with the skill and not checked.** p60 to p81 state no order and no cap inside an Excellency's table. Two rows carry a prerequisite in their own words, Combatant's `Armored for War` ("a prerequisite of medium armor", p70) and Tornado's Included `Bow and Sword` ("a pre-requisite of the Archery skill", p75), as the Ice Domain's `Ice's Skill` already did. The book never says which skills count as medium armor, so the builder shows the sentence and leaves the check to Staff. Reverse it for `Bow and Sword` alone by adding a problem in `priceBuild` when Tornado is held without `open-skills/open-skills/archery`.

**#843. "You can purchase this skill twice" is two purchases at the printed CP each.** Forge fire's `Heat the Forge` (p61) and Ordinator's `Extended Healing` (p72) are the only two Excellency rows that say it; `TWICE_SKILLS` names them, step 7 gives each a second box, and a third is `duplicate-selection`. The book prints one Cost for the row and no second price, so the second costs the same 3. The Open skill `Quick Reflexes` ("up to 3 times", p83) is still sold once; that is in `Numina/WISHLIST.md`. Reverse it by emptying `TWICE_SKILLS`.

**#844. "You can purchase this skill up to 3 times" is up to three purchases at the printed CP each.** `Quick Reflexes` (Open skills, p83) is the only row that says it, and it prints one Cost, 1, and no second price, so each purchase is 1 CP, the same reading #843 gave "twice". `PURCHASE_LIMITS` in `build-rules.js` holds the number of purchases a row allows (2 for the two `TWICE_SKILLS`, 3 for this one), step 7 gives the row three boxes, and a fourth is `duplicate-selection`. A build holds it as one to three entries in `openSkills`, the list and fragment key it always had, so a save or link holding it once is unchanged. Reverse it by taking the row out of `PURCHASE_LIMITS`.

**#845. `Bow and Sword` without `Archery` is a problem, and it is the only printed prerequisite the builder checks.** Tornado's Included row says "(This skill has a pre-requisite of the Archery skill.)" (p75), and the book has one skill of that name, an Open skill at 3 CP with no tiers (p82), so #842's reversal is taken for this row alone. `priceBuild` reports `prerequisite-missing` under step 7, where Archery is sold, when a build has the row and not Archery; the Excellency is still charged its 5, 6 or 7. The row is Included and has no box, so step 7 prints the sentence under Tornado's heading. A save holding Tornado without Archery loads at the CP it had and now carries the problem. "Medium armor" (`Armored for War`, `Ice's Skill`) stays unchecked, as #842 has it. Reverse it by emptying `PREREQUISITES`.

**#846. The floor brush's re-bake puts a room's accent walls back by where they were.** A stroke redraws every room on the lattice with new rings, and `paint.js` put the doorways back and dropped `ring.accents`, so one stroke anywhere took every accent off the storey. `reapplyAccents` gives a new segment the accent that lay along at least half of it, on the same line and running the same way (`accentSpans`, `spanOverlap` in `shapes.js`); the other way is the wall's far face. Half exactly counts. The trace has no corner in a straight wall of one kind, so two colours on one such wall come back as the longer one, or as none when neither covers half. Reverse it by deleting the `reapplyAccents` loop in `paintCells`.

**#847. Reserved: #818 to #847 are held for sessions on Huginn (the Selector loop); anyone else numbers from #848.**

**#848. A game that ships no `.glb` stands its own sprite builders on the plinth: `dioramas.json`'s `build` runs the game's script with its own `common.py`, and its unlit materials become lit ones of the same colour.** Faire Weekend's `markers.py`, Absalom's `tiles.py` and `figures.py` and Corner & Kettle's `cups.py` already build their pictures as geometry and render it flat, so modelling those games a second time for a diorama would put two drawings of each thing in the repo. `diorama.py` loads the script with its project's `common.py` under the name `common`, drops a top-level `main()` call (`markers.py` and `cups.py` have no `__main__` guard), and swaps `flat`, `face_mat` (Absalom's top colour stands for all three faces, since the lamps now shade them), `holdout_mat`, `holdout` and `bare` for lit materials or nothing. `colours` recolours a material by name, for the two things a game tints at draw time: Absalom's heir mask takes the vault wizard's `#3f6ea8`, a cup's white liquid a mix the game would make. No project file changed. `dioramas.mjs` fails a `build` whose script or `common.py` is missing, a call the script neither defines nor lists in `BUILDERS`, and a placement off the plinth. Reverse by giving each card a `place` list of exported `.glb` files. *The site, dioramas.*

**#849. A 2D game's diorama is its own board built from its own pieces, laid out by the game's rules.** Faire Weekend is a plat: the path, a hill, a woods corner, the gate at the path's west end, a stage and four stalls, none of them on the hill because the game refuses a stall there, and the demo camp on it. Absalom is one room of the vault: flagstones in the two floor shades, walls on two sides with the door, two pillars, the casket on its treasure square, a stair square, the heir, two foes and the Keeper. Corner & Kettle is the counter, which the game never draws in 3D, built from `wall` shapes (a `wall` may take `z` now, for the counter's top), with four drinks the cup layers stack and three of the four foods; the customers stay out, since they are a pixel pattern and not geometry (#808). School Generator ships models and is a classroom like Fourth Quarter's bar: twelve desks with their chairs facing the board, the teacher's workstation, lockers, a shelf, a cubby and a plant. Reverse a card by editing its entry. *The site, dioramas.*

**#850. A game with no Blender pipeline builds its diorama from five plain solids in `SHAPES`: `block`, `prism`, `ball`, `dots` and `ring`, and any shape may repeat with `count` and `step`.** Closing Time, Integer Foundry and Daredevil draw in the DOM, so there is nothing of theirs to stand on the plinth and nothing to model a second time. A kind per thing (`house`, `bus`, `forge`) would have been a dozen kinds used once each; five solids that take a size, a turn and a colour build all three scenes and Orbital's marker and flight plan. `prism` puts its ridge `peak` of the way across, so one kind is a gable roof (0.5) and both of Daredevil's ramps (1 and 0). A copy past the first is named `-k`, so the eight done cards build exactly as before. `dioramas.mjs` now holds a shape's whole footprint to the plinth top (the corners of a turned `size`, a ball's radius, a ring's tick ends), at its first copy and its last, where it used to check only `at`; the old check passed a house widened past the edge and a row of 20 buses. It also fails a solid missing a field `diorama.py` reads, which would otherwise be a KeyError on Devon's machine only. Reverse by deleting the five and the cards that use them. *The site, dioramas.*

**#851. Orbital stands `bodies.py`'s own bodies on brass orrery posts, and the star takes its gradient's dark stop, `#c9701a`, for its base.** `bodies.py` has a `__main__` guard and builds each body from `(rng, colour)`, so a `build` call passes `{"rng": SEED + i}`, the seed its sheet frame uses, and `{"palette": key}`, read from `COLOR` in `js/render.js` by the game's own `palette()`. Its `common.py` makes Workbench materials through `material()`, colour in `diffuse_color` only, which Cycles ignores, so `relight()` swaps `material` too; no other project's `common.py` defines one. `glow` gives a material an emission strength, for the rings and chevrons the game draws as neon. Lit under AgX and the warm key, `#ffb257` came out peach with or without glow, beside a planet that read as its own cyan; the stop drawBody already uses is a game colour, not a new one, and reads as the game's orange. The marker, probe and dotted flight plan are `ring`, `ball` and `dots` in render.js's `#5dffa6`, `#fff6e0` and `#ffcf7a`. Reverse the star by deleting its `colours`. *The site, dioramas, Orbital.*

**#852. A DOM game's diorama is the place its story happens.** Closing Time is a Carver Mill street: a cape, a two-storey, a brick rancher and one more, lit windows, For Sale signs in `--sign-red` (the rancher's with a SOLD rider), the mill and its chimney behind, the agent's car at the kerb. Integer Foundry is its board: an 8 by 6 grid of tiles, two lines from teal sources along belts through forges to glowing sinks, copper ingots on the belts and stacked bars on the back row. Daredevil is Milestone 4 side-on: the 36-degree launch ramp and 18-degree landing from `stunt.js`'s `GEO`, seven school buses between them, Duke over the gap, a three-tier grandstand and the cold open's water tower. Every colour is the game's CSS. Reverse a card by editing its entry. *The site, dioramas.*

**#853. `dioramas.mjs --rendered` leaves the frame's outermost pixel ring unread and takes a row's backdrop from the median of its pixels 1 to 5, not from pixel 0.** Torchbearer's full render failed "sits inside frame.safe" at [0.001, 0.000, 0.999, 0.803]: its pixel (0,0) came out 32,24,15 among neighbours at 39,25,12, the denoiser's edge padding, and as row 0's one reference it made the whole top row read as diorama. Fracture Cycle's corner was 33 and passed by one count. All 14 renders pass the new check, and a 4 px dot painted at (60,60) of a copy still fails the same line at [0.023, 0.037, ...]. Reverse by reading from pixel 0 again with a wider `tolerance`. *The site, dioramas.*

**#854. The Fracture Cycle's diorama is the seam: the title screen's own crack laid front to back across the plinth in `--ley`, Radiant ground west and Dire east, and everything the story puts on it.** The Radiant Ancient in `--parchment` with gold posts and a crown, the Dire Ancient's red spikes round a `--dire-text` core, the empty socket in the middle with roots from both soils and the three fragments over it, Carl's Unclaimed Tower on the seam at the back with ley runes, Roseleaf's two stalls and their lanterns at the front, and the nameless soul before the socket. The crack is the SVG's `M10,32 ... L210,32` path scaled to the plinth, so `patch` takes `emit` now. `--radiant` `#e8c468` went cream when lit or glowing; at `metallic` 0.5 to 0.8 it reads as the game's gold, and the Dire fragment stays unlit because the text says it does not glow. *The site, dioramas.*

**#855. Torchbearer's diorama is Act II's "The Tower in the Water", from the bundled Bell of Barrowmoor.** The black mere in `--soot`, glossy, over all but the dry moor at the front left. Saint Verity's sunk to its shoulders with roof beams like ribs, the bell tower leaning ten degrees with a verdigris bell, the yard on its hummock with its stones tilted, the drowned causeway, two peat-black hounds and the rusted dead on the hummock, a knot of corpse-light over the graves, reeds, and the torchbearer at the causeway's foot in `--ember` with a `--brass` flame. The flame started in `--ember-hi` and read salmon under AgX; `--brass` and `--brass-hi` read as fire. The reeds started as `count` rows and read as combs, so each is its own shape from a seeded generator. Reverse a card by editing its entry. *The site, dioramas.*

**#856. The design diff matches an accent wall by its line, and says "gained", "lost" or "had an accent wall repainted".** A ring is renumbered by any edit that reshapes it, so `designdiff.js` compares `accentSpans` and not `ring.accents[i]`: an accent after that overlaps one before by more than 0.5ft on the same line and direction is that accent. A room whose accent moved to another wall reads as one gained and one lost. The clauses sit in the room's sentence beside "gained a door" and "was refinished", and the mark is the room's. Reverse it by removing `accentChanges` from `compareRoom`.

**#857. The accent brush refuses a face with no wall on its line.** #827 lets a ring hold an accent on a `SEG_NONE` segment because the wall may be the neighbour's, and so a click could store a colour that paints nothing. `wallAlongSeg` in `wallrun.js` asks for a built segment on that line in any ring, or a free-standing wall, within 0.01ft (`finish.js`'s tolerance, not the wall tool's 0.9ft), and the brush says "has no wall on that side to paint" and stores nothing. Taking an accent off is never refused, and `setSegAccent` and the file reader are unchanged, so a file that holds such an accent still loads with it. A wall erased after its accent was painted leaves the accent in the file. Reverse it by removing the check in `accentPointerDown`.

**#858. A browser check that follows a closed `<dialog>` waits for what the close handler does, not for the dialog to be gone.** Chromium closes a dialog when `close()` returns and fires its `close` event with the next frame (measured: after a 0 ms timer, beside `requestAnimationFrame`, 3 to 13 ms later). `Numina/test/a11y/search.mjs` asked where focus was in that gap and failed twice over, three times in CI on 2026-10-04. The page is right and is unchanged; the test polls for the focus, with no fixed sleep and no retry.

**#859. An erased wall takes its accents with it, at the eraser and at load.** #857 stopped a colour being stored on a face with no wall and left the other way in: paint the wall, then erase it. `pruneAccents` in `wallrun.js` takes off every accent on the storey whose line has no wall by #857's own test (`wallAlongSeg`), so the brush would refuse exactly what it removes. It is asked of the storey and not of the cleared segment because the wall may be another ring's or free-standing: erasing a partition takes the accent off both rooms' faces, and an accent stays while any wall is left on its line. It runs where a wall is removed: `eraseSegWall` (the eraser's click and its stroke), `eraseWallLineAt`, the eraser on a free-drawn room and Delete on selected rooms. `setSegWall` in `shapes.js` still keeps the accent, since it cannot see the storey. `readFloor` runs it on every load after the rooms and wall lines are both read, which is `repair` and not `migrate` (#37): a file an older build wrote with such an accent loads without it, as a doorway on a segment with no wall always has, and `SAVE_VERSION` stays 12. The design diff needed nothing: the room reads "lost an accent wall" through #856. An edit that moves a wall off an accent's line without erasing it (a dragged corner) still leaves the accent until the next load. Reverse it by emptying `pruneAccents`.

**#885. Reserved: #856 to #885 are held for sessions on Huginn (the Selector loop); anyone else numbers from #886.**

# The log

One paragraph per phase or batch, oldest first within each project. PR and
decision numbers point into the sections above and into git history.

## The site sessions 1 to 5 (versions 2 to 6)

Session 1 fixed four broken board links, built the wax-seal glyph sprite, the ledger rail and the version line. Session 2 ran a real browser for the first time, found Castle Conundrum broken in production and landed `tools/board-check/`. Session 3 fixed Castle Conundrum's hang and ran board-check clean on Windows. Session 4 vendored three.js into each project's `libs/` and made `harness.mjs` work on Windows. Session 5 mapped the NPC models and turned a playthrough into `npm run play`. Decisions cited: #1, #13, #14, #17, #23.

## The site sessions 6 to 10 (versions 7 to 11)

Session 6 captured all seven previews, added favicons and OG tags, and gave `interaction.js` line of sight. Session 7 made The Fourth Quarter the first `gvb-save.js` adopter and added `npm run games` (94 checks). Session 8 deleted the Bestiary Gallery (3,894 hotlinked images) and took the save module to eleven adopters. Session 9 fixed a direct-commit breakage and a `puppeteer-core` incompatibility. Session 10 fixed the CRLF false-DRIFT in `sync-social-tags.mjs`; `npm run games` went fully green at 146 checks. Lesson: verify a guard-rail by reintroducing its bug (#34).

## Versions 12 to 15

No handoff exists. The repository's history starts at a squashed import on 2026-08-25 with the footer already at version 15, so what sessions 11 to 14 shipped is unknown. Do not guess.

## The prompt rounds, round 1 (site version 8 to 9)

The first parallel round: 22 per-project prompts running at once, one closing prompt applying shared-file requests. Headline finds: Daredevil had never been completable (five wiring bugs, fixed), The Absalom Inheritance was unwinnable at 0 of 2,000 runs (fixed to 59.3%), Final Grade Checker rounded at .45 instead of .5 (fixed), Integer Foundry dealt unfillable orders from order 12 (BFS solver), and Torchbearer's Fighter could never Shield Block. `gvb-save.js` went from one adopter to eleven.

## The prompt rounds, round 2 (site version 9 to 10)

All twenty threads shipped. Final Grade Checker's second grading bug (quality points do not round at .5), an Absalom stall bug in the new Reliquary that only seeded balance runs caught, Daredevil's 356 KB monolith split into four files, a Schedule Visualizer PDF export cut from 21 MB to about 190 KB, and one repo-wide `puppeteer-core` fix. Devon settled three standing questions: Pathfinder pages harmonize rather than share, the committed schedule data stays, and percentages round at .5 while quality points do not.

## The prompt rounds, round 3 (site version 10 to 11)

Every thread shipped. Final Grade Checker's correct quality-point thresholds are whole numbers (4/3/2/1/0), so every `x.75` average had been a letter high for the tool's whole history. Daredevil found its biggest open item: refusing Earl barely changes milestones M2 to M4. Schedule Visualizer's 863,737-byte file became a shell plus a seven-file `Tools/schedule/app/`, verified byte for byte. Orbital got a physics suite covering all 22 levels. The false-DRIFT reported by sixteen projects was root-caused once.

## What the round system got wrong, and what it got right

Two founding facts were false and were corrected in place: the site did make offsite requests (fixed by #44's static sweep), and the suites only drove the seven games (fixed by deleting the Bestiary Gallery and adding `npm run tools`). What outlived the system is the "Questions for Devon" block, now in `BACKLOG.md`. The closing prompt refused to write a handoff from partial notes, by design, and did so in round 2. The fourth round never started.

## School Generator, phases 1 to 18

No per-phase record survives for Phases 1 to 11 because history begins at a squashed import. Phases 12 to 18 are known only by their commit names: the lattice and save v11 (12), the drawing sheet (13), two-person editing (14), a school day (15), cost (16), the meshed site (17) and the server that never shipped (18).

## School Generator, arc four: the guest (Phases 19 to 24)

Shipped a first-visit opening, a Ctrl-K command palette, a coach, a phone layout, five time-of-day moods and lit troffers, `sightline.js` gating walk-mode labels on line of sight, carrying props in walk mode, a shareable walk bundle, and a lights-out night. Phase 19 found the tool never booted on a phone at all. Lesson: sight has its own idea of a wall, so `sightSegments` derives its own occluders rather than reusing `collide.js`'s `wallSegments` (which counts glass as solid and cuts no windows).

## School Generator, arc five: the living school (Phases 25 to 36)

Shipped free-standing `floor.walls`, a press-or-drag eraser, baked light that stops at walls, spatial audio, `weather.js`, a service worker and first-run demo, surface finishes, repeated rooms, directed tours, a readable history, and zoom-following grid snap for every tool. Phase 31 measured transmission at 547 ms to 1,186 ms a frame and moved refraction to photo mode. The TDZ bug (a `let` read above its declaration) shipped three times. Lesson: a phase's premise is checked against the tree, not the wishlist (Phase 32).

## School Generator, arc six: the design review (Phases 37 to 42)

Shipped multi-sheet drawings, dimensions printed on the sheet, arrival and dismissal through real doors, a wheelchair walker with `clearance.js`, code editions in `codes.js` with `commonpath.js`, and a boot diet that took 312 KB and twelve requests off first load. Phase 40's clearance pass found real 33 in pinches in the corridor template. Twelve struck-through backlog items were closed by named phases; three landed partly and stay in the wishlist. Lesson: the walk bundle's cycle check, not runtime, is what catches import cycles.

## Hearth, sprints 4 to 16

A zero-dependency island that round-trips through the URL hash. The sprints added wildlife, blessings and hash saves (with a variable-width 9 to 16 bit LZW that cut the payload to 55%), sound, the mill, prayer and arcs, heirlooms, the walking of the bounds, and songs and news. Recurring traps: `//` comments eating one-line statements, version-pinned asserts in older harness modes, and `rnd()` leaking into audio or click paths. Lesson: assert on durable signals (the chronicle, counts), not transient state or log text.

## Hearth, Phases 6 and 7

Phase 6's first increment added the short winter: a reckoning, rations, a granary raid, an elder who eats last, save v14 and a `strain` mode (#68, #69, #71). Phase 7 cleared five leftovers named by sprint after sprint: a clock-phased play ring, elders telling of the dead they outlived, a weighted prayer draw, and a named birth-cap constant (#79 to #82), with forty-day `pack()` hashes unchanged (#74). Lesson: when an older check goes red on a shifted stream, suspect the check first (#66, six instances).

## Hearth, Phase 8

`hearth-ci.yml` runs `determinism`, `save`, a twelve-day `soak` and a new `pinned` mode on every PR, the other fifteen modes nightly (#83, #84). `pinned` compares forty-day `pack()` hashes against `test/hashes.json` (`41eb3cb5:37275` on seed 7, `4ac7d23d:36964` on 20260819). Lesson: V8's `Math.pow`, `Math.sin` and `Math.cos` differ between releases, so Chromium 151 ran a different island, and the lockfile pins Playwright 1.56.1 exactly.

## The Fourth Quarter, Phases 1 to 3 (PRs #170, #172, #174, #176)

Phase 1 made each room a pure description in `js/layout.js` and held the derivation to a Corner Tap fixture (#182, #184). Phase 2 added three bigger rooms on a 30/44/58/76-seat ladder, annexes (Midtown's back room) and the flagship's mezzanine with `floorYAt()` and one step rule (#185 to #188, #194 to #200). Phase 3 gave every walker an A* nav grid with string-pull and `Route` (#189 to #193). Measured penetration into furniture: 0.000 m in all rooms. Node total 509 to 734.

## The Fourth Quarter, Phases 4 and 5 (PR #178)

Phase 5 put `smoke-engine.mjs`, `smoke-campaign.mjs` and every other `test/*.mjs` on `fourth-quarter-ci.yml` for each PR. Phase 4 cut 27 Poly Haven 2K textures (69,218,191 bytes) to a checked-in 1k set of 5,076,840 bytes, chosen by `pickTier()` with a `?tex=` override (#201, #202): 5.02 s to fully textured at 20 Mbps against 30.86 s. Lesson (#147): counting texture requests on the wire stayed green against a broken cache because Chromium's memory cache answers silently, so the check reads the `LoadingManager` count.

## The Fourth Quarter, Phases 6 and 7 (PRs #179, #183, #185)

Phase 6 added `js/league.js`: an eight-team MAFA double round-robin, calendar as arithmetic on the day, one result shared by TV, box score and standings (#203 to #205). Phase 7 ported regulars, reputation and The End Zone rival (#207 to #211), then put regulars on the floor with nameplates, their usual pre-filled and a first-round comp on E (#212 to #214). Node total 924 to 1,153. Lesson: `freshName()` had two lines guarding one absence, so deleting either stayed green; it now picks from the 300 open names.

## The Fourth Quarter, Phases 8 and 9 (PR #187, Phase 9 PR pending)

Phase 8 put nineteen of the 2D build's 21 event cards on the floor: pure `js/events.js`, `js/moments.js` for the in-room person or prop, a "Night's Moments" box-score section, `eventCd` cooldowns in the save (#215 to #218). Phase 9 made a night losable: `LEASE_STRIKES` nights below `LEASE_FLOOR` evict you one rung down, and only eviction from the Corner Tap sets `failed` (#219 to #222). Node total 1,153 to 1,326. Lesson: three breaks first left the suite green, including dropped upkeep invisible on a campaign owning no gear.

## Faire Weekend, stages 1–22
Stages 1 to 9 built the game: plots, performers, vendors, events, the plan/gates/report loop, terrain-derived `quoteBuild()`, contracts, season, expansion. Stages 11 to 18 added placement rules, 2x2 stages, paths, upkeep, reachability BFS, win/loss. Stage 19 found an empty field earned +$5,420/day and `state.builtPlots` never touched attendance; `computeGroundsDraw` fixed it and the `SIGNIFICANCE:` test class was born. Stage 21 made a day final once gates close (#45) and vendored fonts; Stage 22 adopted `gvb-save.js`. Lesson: build a fresh `createSaveSlot()` per simulated reload in Node suites.

## Faire Weekend, Phase 1: guests who walk and the economy (PR #191, PR #193)
Increment 1 added `GUESTS` archetypes and pure `js/guests.js`: at most 400 sampled agents walk path routes toward attractions with a repeat penalty; the economy stayed put (#223 to #225). Increment 2 made the walk the only source of stall money via `spentAt` and `represents`, ruled the col-3 spur unbuildable, cut `wristbandCut` to 0.12, let the gate take its purse share first and set `stepsPerBlock` to 6 (#226 to #230). SIGNIFICANCE 8 to 10 added. Lesson: scale the buyer count once and price the gross off it.

## Faire Weekend, Phase 2: weather worth checking (PR #195)
Seven-row `WEATHER` table multiplying block heat, gate and mood, with a season weight ramp across `WEATHER_SEASON_SPAN` weekends. Weather is a pure function of a per-save `weatherSeed` and calendar position, so tomorrow's forecast is computable and reloads are stable (#231); `createInitialState` takes a seed and only `newGame()` draws one (#232). HUD slot, Office forecast card, weather-scaled heat pips and a stub row shipped; SIGNIFICANCE 11 asserts shade vs hilltop on mood, not net. `smoke.mjs` 857 to 1,118.

## Faire Weekend, Phase 3: acts with a story (PR #197)
`state.relationships[id]` 0 to 100 per contracted act, moved by what the day did, five tiers, deleted on release. Eight `ARCS` subjects with sixteen beats resolved by `resolveBeat`; `performerFor`/`vendorFor` overlay traits. `quoteContract` is the one rate computation, with inline negotiation of commitment and break fee. `evt_encore` and `evt_late_call` gate on Devoted and Sour. Decisions #235 to #240; SIGNIFICANCE 12 and 13. `smoke.mjs` 1,118 to 1,652.

## Faire Weekend, Phase 4: a faire that outlives its season
Renown as a second track from `weekendRenown` (#241); `closeSeason` banks a `seasonRecord` and opens the next run via `carryoverPreview` (#242, #244); the save slot went to version 2 with the first real `migrateSave` (#243). South Meadow tier and the Gilded Company unlock off renown (#245). Finding for Q27: a scripted manager banked $28,000 to $104,000 but never took reputation past 63, so the reputation bar is out of reach. `smoke.mjs` 1,652 to 1,825.

## Faire Weekend, Phase 5: the layout review
`tools/shoot-states.mjs` photographs seventeen states at four viewports and records live rectangles; it asserts nothing. It measured the Fair Floor tab 1,820px wide at 1280, a 187px brown slab beside the map and a 191px phone HUD. Rulings #246 to #250: plat column as a `calc()` off `--cols`, map `max-content`, tables in `.table-scroll`, touch sizes on `(pointer: coarse)` not width (answering Q29 and Q30). Phone HUD now 137px. Lesson: a block-level grid is its container's width whatever its tracks add up to.

## Faire Weekend, Phase 6: pannable map and build preview (PR #203, PR #205)
Increment 1 replaced the terrain div grid with a canvas: pure `js/mapview.js` geometry, `js/plat.js` painting, DOM markers riding the same transform (#251, #252), drag, pinch, Ctrl+wheel, keys. Increment 2 added pure `previewPlacement()` returning draw deltas, reach and `drops` or a refusal reason (#253), shown in a `role="status"` `.plat-readout` (#254), plus `tools/touch-readout.mjs` (`npm run touch`) under real coarse-pointer Chromium. Lesson: Playwright's `hasTouch` leaves `(pointer: coarse)` false, so touch checks need Blink's flags.

## Faire Weekend, Phase 7: a third crew
`CREW`, six rows over three roles with a `covers` field. The gate is a 550-guest ceiling that gate crews raise (#255); crew are staff with no relationship or renown (#256); the watch scales incident events by uncovered crowd exposure (#257); one `contractedCost()` path (#258); the herald moves overflow measured against `CROWDING_FILL` (#259); Section 1g fixtures had never scheduled an act (#260). The full-run manager now wins season one at reputation 74, relevant to Q27. `smoke.mjs` 1,951 to 2,108.

## Faire Weekend, Phase 8: the wiring audit, automatic
`tests/wiring.mjs` (136 checks) replaces the by-hand grep: every emitted `data-action` must be handled and every handler emitted and exercised by `smoke.mjs` or `play-games.mjs`. First run found `contractCrew` and `releaseCrew` never clicked. It is its own file so it cannot satisfy itself (#262), resolves the interpolated contract-button emitter off call sites (#263), and keeps an allowlist checked from both ends (#264). No game code changed.

## Daredevil arc one, Phase 1 increment 1 (2026-09-10)
Declining Earl at the fair had changed nothing. Shape B (#265): a self-financed Milestone 2 on `rels.earl === 'absent'`, fourteen `m2_solo_*` scenes (promoter, bank, Duke's arithmetic), six new flags including `soloM2`, and a backer-less Free Roam 2 where `fr2_debt_01` must be played first (#266). `triggerStatUpdate` accepts a function for `reason`. New `no_earl_solo` transcript; `smoke-page.mjs` plays a full "Not interested" run to Milestone 5.

## Daredevil arc one, Phase 1 increment 2 (2026-09-10)
From `m3_entry` on, the solo run still read 31 lines of Earl. Decisions #267 to #269: every such line reads a `solo()` helper and names who is there instead (Kessler, Petersen, Cal) across M3, FR3, M4, FR4, M5 and the epilogue; "Earl picks" is hidden. Choice text, subtext and stat-update titles may be functions of state. `smoke-page.mjs` now asserts no `\bEarl\b` from `m3_entry` to the ending; sixth transcript `no_earl_crash` added.

## Daredevil arc one, Phase 2 (2026-09-11)
Four finished pieces of writing were unreachable and two flags could never be true. Decisions #270 to #274: both Free Roam closes routed on both branches, `m2_sign` actually sets `rels.earl` and `m2Complete`, `m5StuntFlags()` makes the Earl-picked ending reachable, one relationship label table, `pressAtFair` cut, and the mentor ending credits Pete. New `test/flags.mjs` (7 assertions) freezes a 28-name write-only list. Lesson: a scene nothing routes to is not an error, so it needs a static reachability check.

## Daredevil arc one, Phase 3 (2026-09-11)
Relationships became a declared table. Decisions #275 to #278: `pete` seeded as `'unknown'`, `CAST` in leaf module `js/cast.js`, route tables walked in their own order by `routeByCast()`, and `setRel()`/`repairState` refusing and repairing illegal states. `_needs` lists built by `statesOf()`/`presentStates()` replace relationship `_requires` closures. `smoke-save.mjs` 53 to 110 sweeps 135 (character, state) mentions. Lesson: the unreachable `ruthie === 'warm'` stayed green three rounds because a comparison against a state nobody reaches is not an error.

## Daredevil arc one, Phase 4 (2026-09-11, PR #220)
Seven of 26 cast states were written by nothing. Decisions #279 to #282: Tommy's three-evening track gated by `tommyAsked`, the debt arm demoting flat, Danny's `poached`/`absent` from `fr3_danny`, and `rosterFor()` for the ending roster. `smoke-save.mjs` gained a state-reachability check with three dead states frozen. Measured, not fixed: choice-reached scenes with a `statUpdate` apply deltas twice (33 frozen); new scenes put numbers in `effects`. Nine transcripts; `smoke-page.mjs` 93 to 136.

## Daredevil arc one, between Phases 4 and 5 (2026-09-11)
Two sessions built Phase 4 the same day because each claimed rank 1 on its own branch. PR #220 shipped and #221 re-ranked; PR #222 was closed unmerged and nothing from it carried forward. Decision #283: a claim counts only once it is merged to `main`, now written into `BACKLOG.md`'s Tier 1 preamble and the root `CLAUDE.md`.

## Daredevil arc two, Phase 5: a walker that knows what it did not reach (2026-09-11)
Shipped `test/graph.mjs`, which builds the story graph from `SCENES` data and `engine.js` text (232 scenes, 24 routes, 412 edges) and walks it plainly and over reachable relationship bags (103,443 states). It found one dead route and one unreachable scene. Decisions #284 to #285. `smoke-save.mjs` went 127 to 134. Lesson: reading the engine as text over-approximates on purpose, so the tool can miss an orphan but never invent one.

## Daredevil arc two, Phase 6: evenings that cost something (2026-09-11)
Added `js/money.js`, a dependency-free hub economy: budgets below card counts, priced evenings, per-hub take, notes via `monthlyOn()`, the $900 bus deposit on the solo branch, and cost tags on every card through one `renderHubShelf()`. The ending screen reads the books back as a fifth verdict. Decisions #286 to #289. `smoke-save.mjs` 134 to 199, 18 planted breaks. Lesson: the double-applied `statUpdate` was found by a transcript, not code, so running totals only move through `effects`.

## Daredevil arc two, Phase 7: three stunts that are three stunts (2026-09-11)
Added `js/stunt.js`: one count per scale derives gap, run-up, top end, landing zone, tolerance and drift, and launch speed is solved from geometry. Retry costs a Condition point once, the Scale pill row is gone, the Recovery result is read, crowd work can fail, and the autopilot aims at the run's own tier. Decisions #290 to #293. `smoke-save.mjs` 199 to 246. Lesson: with the old autopilot constant, cars and buses failed, which is the proof the three scales now differ.

## Daredevil arc two, Phase 8: a workflow that runs the suite (2026-09-11)
Shipped `daredevil-ci.yml`: a fast Node `suite` job, then `browser` (`smoke-page.mjs`, `verify-touch-375.mjs`) and a nine-way `transcripts` matrix, both `needs: suite`. The path filter includes `assets/js/gvb-save.js` and board-check's `harness.mjs` plus lockfile. `transcript.mjs --check` makes transcripts an assertion. Decisions #294 to #296. Closes arc two.
